# L73 on device images: the RFC 9887 client against port49 — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Measure every cell (a)–(j) of spec §15 L73 against port49d's `tls_listener` on four required platforms, one per vendor, in this order: vJunos-router 26.2R1.7, Catalyst 8000V (IOS XE) 17.18.02, Nexus 9300v (NX-OS) 10.6.2.F and cEOS-lab (EOS) 4.36.3M. Each platform's run begins with an L71 cell: it reads the image's own CLI help to find out whether the image has an RFC 9887 client. Each platform gets its own L73 results note. Two more images get the L71 help cell only: IOL-XE 17.18.02 and XRd 26.1.1. vJunos-switch 26.2R1.7 runs last; it is measured, but closing the row does not wait on it. If all four required platforms pass, the last task closes L73 (CLAUDE.md lab rule 3). Otherwise the row stays open and records what was measured.

**Architecture:** The device side reuses the containerlab and vrnetlab harness (`tests/lab/harness_test.go`).
- **Bring-up and reconfiguration.** The device comes up over plain TACACS+. A break-glass CLI then reconfigures it, with port49d stopped.
- **Platform rows.** The new rows are opt-in and sit on the existing node identities: `srx1` (.21), `xe1` (.41), `nx1` (.31) and `leaf1` (.11). The inventory and the startup configs therefore need no change.
- **Dialects.** One harness serves every platform through a small *dialect* table (`l73Dialects`, untagged and unit-tested). A dialect holds what the platform's help is asked, the lines that point its client at port49 and put it back, its enrolment dialogue, and the commands that read its version, log and configuration.
- **Server side.** The server is the lab's port49d, restarted for each cell from a derived config that adds a `tls_listener:` block and marks the device `transport: tls`.
- **Device certificate.** The device makes its own key pair. The harness signs the device's certificate request with a lab intermediate. Only public certificates go onto the device: by scp on Junos and EOS, pasted at the enrolment prompt on IOS XE and NX-OS.
- **Captures and evidence.** Each cell's raw capture is git-ignored. The committed evidence is a per-connection TLS summary (`<cell>.tls.json`), built so that it cannot carry session material. It sits next to:
  - the redacted decision log;
  - the device's masked configuration and TLS log lines;
  - the masked CLI help exchange.
- **Tests.** Unit-testable code is untagged and runs under `make test`. The live test is `//go:build lab`.

**Tech Stack:**
- Go 1.26 (`crypto/tls`, `crypto/x509`, `encoding/asn1`).
- containerlab.
- vrnetlab (`~/git/vrnetlab`, commit `3a34fa6`): `juniper/vjunosrouter`, `cisco/c8000v`, `cisco/n9kv`, `cisco/iol`, `juniper/vjunosswitch`.
- cEOS-lab as an imported docker image, `ceos:4.36.3M` (containerlab kind `arista_ceos`).
- `docker load` for XRd.
- The cmattoon/guestfish container, for the AMD CPU-check patch.
- tcpdump, and the lab's `labpcap` reader.

**Spec:** `docs/design/2026-08-23-policy-engine-spec.md`:
- §15 rows L71 (line 11166), L72 (11167) and L73 (11168).
- §13 decision 50 (lines 10125–10385), rulings 1, 2, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11, 13, 15 and 19.

**Device documents the dialects follow:**
- Juniper, "TACACS+ over TLS overview" (26.2R1).
- Cisco doc 225097, "Configure TACACS+ over TLS on IOS XE" (17.18).
- Cisco doc 224882, "Configure TACACS+ over TLS on NX-OS" (10.6).
- Arista EOS User Manual, TACACS+ and "SSL profiles" (`management security` / `ssl profile`), for 4.36.1F and later.

Read `CLAUDE.md`, `tests/lab/README.md`, `tests/lab/results/L71-*.md` and `tests/lab/results/L72-reference.md` before Task 1.

## Global Constraints

- Go 1.26+, module `github.com/blanketfish/port49`, `gofmt` and `go vet` clean (`go vet -tags lab ./tests/lab/` too), no cgo.
- snake_case everywhere it is data: JSON keys in evidence, YAML keys.
- Reason codes and TLS outcomes come from a closed vocabulary (`internal/decision/reasons.go`, `internal/devicecert/outcome.go`). L73 reads them. It never adds to them and never invents one.
- The log never contains a token, password, challenge or response. Credentials appear only as `credential_sha256`. Evidence carries even that one as `<redacted sha256>` (`l72RedactCredentialHash`).
- CLAUDE.md lab rules:
  - Rule 1: a claim cited to an open entry is unmeasured.
  - Rule 3: a row with one platform measured and others open **stays open**.
  - Rule 4: if a lab result contradicts a doc, the doc is wrong. Fix the doc, cite the results note, and say so in the commit message.
- Branch `test/l73-junos` (the name predates the widening; keep it), worktree `/home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/l73-junos`. Never commit code to `main`.
- Conventional Commits. One commit per task step that says "Commit". Every commit message ends with exactly:
  ```
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  ```
  No `Claude-Session:` line and no claude.ai URL in any commit, PR body, issue or comment.
- Verify before claiming done: `make test && make vet`, and report the real output.
- **One opt-in selector per run.** The harness caches a lab per platform *name* (`labs[p.name]`), and `iosxe1718` shares `cisco_iosxe` with the 17.13 row.

**Lab rules (verbatim, binding on every lab step):**

- Single occupant.
- STOP sentinel `/tmp/port49-STOP-LAB` checked before every cell.
- Disk floor 67584M checked before start.
- Watchdog + stall watchdog.
- Detached run worktree under `.worktrees/` (NOT `/tmp`: nosuid tmpfs, port49d can't bind).
- `make build` first.
- Harness pins `issued_key_store: file`.
- Copy captures out before removing a worktree.
- An INVALID run writes no note.
- Never touch docker containers whose names aren't the lab's (4 grafana/mcp-grafana containers with random names exist).
- Never `pkill -f`.
- Never print credentials, keys, TLS session material (no debug print of session tickets/keys, redacted or not), tokens, passwords.
- Device config shown with `$6$`/`$9$` hashes masked.
- Read logs only through `grep -v -i 'password\|token\|secret\|eyJ'` + `sed -E 's/[A-Za-z0-9_+\/=.-]{24,}/<LONG>/g'`.

**How the harness enforces them:**
- **Single occupant.** `labOccupyForRun` (Task 3) holds the lock from `TestL73`'s start until `TestMain` has torn the topology down. Each image build holds it too (Task 1). A pre-start check refuses when any container labelled `containerlab` is running. It reads names only and touches nothing.
- **STOP sentinel and disk floor.** `labGuardAt(l66StopSentinel, "/", l66DiskFloorMB)` runs at the start and before every cell (`e.guard()`).
- **An INVALID run writes no note.** Nothing is written to `tests/lab/results/` or `tests/lab/evidence/` until every cell has run and the leak gate has passed (`e.note()`). There is one exception: the L71 addendum when an image has no client, which is a valid negative and not an INVALID run.
- **No TLS session material.** The harness never sets `tls.Config.KeyLogWriter`, never decrypts a capture, and keeps no ticket or PSK bytes. port49d never writes its ticket keys (decision 50 ruling 11).
- **Device output.** Device logs, configuration and enrolment screens pass through `l73ScrubLog` (Task 6), which is the shell rule as code, and through `l71MaskExchange` before they enter evidence or an error message.
- **Disk.** Space is freed only in the order the team lead ruled: `go clean -cache` first, then the `controller-17.13.01a` image, then this plan's own earlier L73 images (Task 1).

**Concurrency with plan 11a.** Plan 11a runs in `.worktrees/directory-and-session-stores`. It runs unit tests only until its tasks 14–15.
- **Today.** The lab has no occupancy lock. The only guards are L44's own `winLock` (on its own path) and `startDaemonWith`'s refusal when something already listens on 172.100.100.1:49.
- **This plan.** Task 3 adds `labOccupy("/tmp/port49-lab.lock", …)`, an exclusive non-blocking `flock` that `TestL73` takes before it deploys anything.
- **Plan 11a's change.** Its lab tasks 14–15 should take the same lock. That is the team lead's change to plan 11a, not this plan's.
- **CPU load.** 11a's `make test` may load the CPU during cell (i), which times fallbacks. The harness records `/proc/loadavg` in cell (i)'s observation, so the reader can weigh the timings.

## Rulings (team lead, 2026-10-17)

These were ruled on the plan's first draft. Executors follow them as written.

1. **Scope.** The plan's L73 platforms are vJunos-router, C8000V 17.18.02, N9Kv 10.6.2.F and cEOS 4.36.3M, plus vJunos-switch. Help cells only for IOL-XE and XR. With Junos, IOS XE, NX-OS and EOS measured and passing, the last task closes L73. The switch is not required for that.
2. **Disk.** Before any build, if free < floor + build size, first run `go clean -cache` (regenerable; it freed 29G last time). Only if the gate is still short, remove the docker image `vrnetlab/cisco_c8000v:controller-17.13.01a`. The README marks it as the wrong image, it can be rebuilt, and 17.18.02 replaces it anyway. **The switch is never skipped over disk.**
3. **XRd sysctl.** Accepted: raise `fs.inotify.max_user_instances` at runtime for the XRd cell, and restore the old value afterwards.
4. Every other choice below is accepted.

### The pre-flight's findings (2026-10-17) and how each was settled

The team lead ruled C1–C5 and I1–I6 (items 5–13). Each Minor was accepted unless its item says otherwise.

5. **C1–C3, compile errors.** `l73JunosAsks` uses keyed fields; the enrolment helper `l73Plain(lines ...string)` is renamed `l73Lines`, since Task 4's `l73Plain` type holds the name; the plan's `fileSize` is gone, and `startOn` uses the lab package's (`directory_e2e_test.go`). Every Go block was extracted into a scratch copy of the worktree, `gofmt -l` printed nothing, `go vet ./tests/lab/` and `go vet -tags lab ./tests/lab/` were clean, and the plan's unit tests passed there.
6. **C4, every cell runs on the daemon config it set.** `cli` no longer calls `withAdminCLI`, which restarts port49d on the standing plain config. It stops port49d and starts it again on `e.cfg`, the config the last `daemon()` call recorded (the standing config before the first cell). `startOn` is the only place TestL73 starts port49d. `TestL73DaemonRunsOnlyTheCellsConfig` (Task 8) parses `l73_test.go` and fails on any `withAdminCLI`, `restart`, `awaitTACACS` or `startDaemon` call, on a `startDaemonWith` outside `startOn`, and on a cell that starts a daemon without `ready` or `revive`.
7. **C5, no `restart` and no record wait.** `daemon()` is `stopDaemon` then `startOn`, gated only on the "RFC 9887 listener on" line. **IOS XE's dead-server memory** (awaitTACACS's comment; L10 on 17.13): configuring the device means a break-glass CLI with port49d stopped, after which the C8000V answered logins locally without a packet. So a cell that needs the login to pass calls `ready` after its configuration: break-glass probes, each under its own capture, until the device opens a connection to 172.100.100.1 (3 minutes, then INVALID). It checks a connection, not a record, so cell (a)'s first login, on whatever port the device defaults to, is covered too. A cell whose handshake port49 may refuse (c, each d variant, e, each i row, j1, j2) calls `revive` first: port49d on cell (a)'s config, `ready`, then the cell's own daemon. No CLI is opened between them. After its login the cell calls `reached`: INVALID unless the device opened a connection to port49, because a refusal the device never reached measures nothing. All of this runs on every platform, so no platform's memory is assumed away. The cost is one or two break-glass logins per cell.
8. **I1, mutations that go red.** Task 4 adds `TestL73StreamDropsOverlapsAndStopsAtAGap`, which tests `l73Stream` directly, and `TestL73SummaryTypesHaveNoRoomForSessionBytes`. The second walks every summary type by reflection against a field-and-type allowlist that has no byte strings, so a field able to hold a random, session id, key share, PSK identity, binder or ticket cannot be added unnoticed. The three mutations are: the stream's overlap slice, the session id written into `ALPN` (the reviewer's case; `SNI` is overwritten later), and a `SessionID` field. Each was run in the scratch copy and went red.
9. **I2, lock through teardown.** `labOccupyForRun` keeps the lock in a package variable. A package variable is needed because an unreachable `*os.File` is closed by its finalizer, which would drop the flock. `TestMain` calls `labRelease()` after `teardown()` and `writeEvidence`.
10. **I3, a value-based secret gate.** P7b reads only what the run wrote under `results/` and `evidence/`, and for an appended note only the appended lines. It compares them with the run's own private keys line by line, `labsecret`, `breakglass-only`, and the shapes of a crypt hash, a JWT and a PEM private key. It prints file names only. The help text's word "secret" no longer trips it.
11. **I4, no sudo by hand beyond the XRd sysctl.** P1 uses `ss -ltn`, `flock -n` and `pgrep -a -x` (a listing). It never stops or kills a process this run did not start. The harness's own `sudo -n` calls (tcpdump in `capture`, and `ip addr` in `addSecondAddress`, mirroring `prepareSplit`) are code that predates this plan, and their existing precedent stands (item 16).
12. **I5, a watchdog on one PID.** P3 builds `lab.test` with `go test -c`, runs it from `tests/lab`, and writes `$!` to `lab.pid`. The watchdog and the stall dump act only on that PID, and only while `/proc/PID/exe` is the run's binary. There is no `pgrep -f` or `pkill`.
13. **I6, captures out on every exit path.** P7a archives the captures under a per-attempt stamp in `/mnt/images/port49-l73-2026-10-17/`, before P7d removes the worktree, for PASS, negative, INVALID, stall and deadline alike. Only a PASS replaces the main checkout's copy.
14. **M1 (gofmt), M4 (loadJunos answers only "load this CA certificate"), M5 (j2 is described as a mixed list on a second address; the same-host question is j1's), M6 (an accepted DNS-only or wrong-IP certificate reads **no** against RFC 9887 §3.4), M7 (guards in `cellF`, `cellB` and `note`), M9 (P5 says how to handle a device log line naming a PSK; the gate is never weakened) and M10 (an older addendum for the same image is deleted, and the new one stands): accepted as the reviewer wrote them.
15. **M3, builds under the lock.** Accepted for builds: each recipe takes `flock -n` on the lab lock (Task 1). For the XRd sysctl the finding is ledgered, not changed. Raising `max_user_instances` only raises a limit and cannot disturb a concurrent run. The restore lowers it back to the host's own value after XRd is destroyed.
16. **M2, `sudo -n ip addr add/del 172.100.100.2`: confirmed by the team lead (2026-10-17), on two conditions.** Cells (i) and (j2) need a second server address on the bridge, and this is `prepareSplit`'s existing call. (1) It touches only the lab bridge (`bridgeDevice()`), and only with the lab-range address `l73SecondServer` (172.100.100.2/24). (2) The `del` runs on every exit path. Its `t.Cleanup` is registered before the `add`, so a Fatal, an INVALID or the STOP guard all remove it. On a signal, TestMain's teardown destroys the bridge, and the address goes with it. No other sudo is typed by hand beyond the XRd sysctl.
17. **M8, the enrolment objects stay on the device at cleanup.** These are trustpoints, keys, the EOS SSL profile, and the Junos CA profiles and local certificate. The cleanup restores the plain TACACS+ servers only, and the topology is destroyed at the end of the run, so nothing outlives it. The note does not claim otherwise.
18. **The re-review (2026-10-17), R1–R6, all applied.**
    - R1: `ready` returns the reaching probe's connections. If the token login sent nothing, cell (a) reads the default port off the probe, and the note says so.
    - R2: cell (h) starts capture `h` before `ready`, so a single-connection session the probe opened is counted.
    - R3: cell (g) uses `revive`, so login 1 holds no ticket.
    - R4: the gate's hash pattern is `\$[0-9][a-z]?\$[^[:space:]]{8,}`.
    - R5: the value list is unchanged (confirmed).
    - R6: `ready`'s INVALID message names dead-server memory.

## Choices made by this plan (planner, 2026-10-17)

- **Platform order.** The full runs come first, in this order: vJunos-router, C8000V, N9Kv, cEOS. Each gets the full set of cells. Then the help cells for IOL-XE and XRd. vJunos-switch comes last.
  - **Each platform starts with the L71 help cell.** If L71's question finds no client keyword (`tls`, or `ssl-profile` on EOS), the run writes the L71 addendum and stops. That is a valid negative. A missing keyword the harness would type means the run is INVALID.
  - **IOL-XE and XRd are help cells only, even if they list a client.** IOL-XE 17.18.02 is the same IOS XE release and TACACS+ code as the C8000V row, so a full run there would repeat it. An XR client would need a new dialect and a TACACS+ startup config. A positive help cell on either is recorded in its L71 note and named as a follow-up in the roadmap.
  - **XRd, not XRv9k.** The XRv9k launcher defaults to 24 GiB of RAM on this 30 GiB host. XRd's control plane needs `fs.inotify.max_user_instances` ≥ 4000, and the host has 128 (ruling 3).
  - **cJunosEvolved and Cat9kv are skipped.** cJunosEvolved needs its own container bring-up. Cat9kv is IOS XE 17.18.02 again.
  - **cEOS is checked for, not built.** It is already imported as `ceos:4.36.3M`. If the image is missing when its task starts, the task records the fact and skips: it does not fail. The row then stays open.
- **Disk: build each image just before its run.** At plan time `/` had 90375M free against the 67584M floor. Task 1's gate frees space in ruling 2's order. If the gate still fails after that, the third step is to remove this plan's own earlier L73 images, oldest first. Their results are committed, and the README says how to rebuild them. Nothing else is ever removed. The stale `~/git/vrnetlab/cisco/n9kv/n9kv-9300v64-10.4.2.F.qcow2` is a build leftover. It is removed only after `cmp` proves it identical to `/mnt/images/nexus9300v64.10.4.2.F.qcow2`.
- **Enrolment follows each vendor's document.**
  - **Junos:** the offline request (`request security pki generate-key-pair` and `generate-certificate-request`). The certificate is loaded back from files sent by scp.
  - **IOS XE and NX-OS:** terminal enrolment (`crypto pki enroll` / `crypto ca enroll`). The request is read off the screen, and the certificate is pasted back at the import prompt.
  - **EOS:** `security pki key generate` and `security pki certificate generate signing-request`, with the request read off the screen. The certificates are copied to `flash:` by scp, then copied into `certificate:`. The SSL profile names the intermediate as its `chain certificate`, as Arista documents; the note says so.
  - **Everywhere:** the private key never leaves the device. SCEP, CMPv2, EST and ACME are not measured.
- **Device-side revocation checking is off:** `revocation-check disable` on Junos, `revocation-check none` on IOS XE and NX-OS, and no CRL in the EOS SSL profile. The lab's CAs publish no CRL, and whether a device checks revocation is not an L73 cell.
- **The device's certificate is issued by an intermediate,** so cell (e) can tell whether the device sends its chain.
- **port49d's settings in every cell:**
  - `certificate_status` is `{disabled: true}` except in cell (i)'s decline window. The status path is L72's, already measured.
  - `malformed_backoff_base` is `2s`.
  - `session_attributes` is the platform's lab value: `[]` on Junos, `["priv-lvl=15"]` on IOS XE, and the lab file's on NX-OS and EOS.
- **ECDSA P-256 PKI first.** If the control login fails, the run is INVALID, and its message says to re-run with `PORT49_L73_ALG=rsa`.
- **A latent L71 bug is fixed in passing** (Task 7). `l71Says` read only the first field of a help line, so Junos's container marker (`> tls`) would have read as no `tls` option. No closed L71 note is affected: none of the committed L71 transcripts contains the word `tls`. Task 17 checks this with `grep -w`.

## Questions for Ryan

None. The disk and sysctl questions were ruled by the team lead (Rulings).

## The operator at 3am

L73 is what plan 12 turns into each vendor's reference configuration, so every cell is read as the 3am question it answers:

- (a): which port the device dials when nobody set one.
- (d): whether a mis-issued server certificate is refused by the device, or silently accepted.
- (e): what an operator's CA must put in the certificate.
- (i): when port49 refuses, whether the device gets to its local method, how fast, and whether it gets there at all.
- (j): whether a TLS failure sends the device's password to a plain-TACACS+ server.

## Review Focus

1. **A device that never connects.** Either the default port has no listener, or the device refuses the server before sending anything.
   - Cell (a) must report "no connection", never a port of 0. `TestL73FirstPortNeedsAConnection` pins this (Task 4).
   - The control login must make the run INVALID rather than produce notes. Task 8's INVALID path covers this.
2. **Session material reaching evidence.** That means the ClientHello's legacy session id, the randoms, the key shares, a resumption's PSK identity and binders.
   - The summary types hold none of them.
   - `l73SummaryLeaks` is a second gate.
   - `TestL73ResumptionSummaryHoldsNoSessionBytes` pins both, and `TestL73SummaryTypesHaveNoRoomForSessionBytes` pins the types' field list (Task 4).
3. **`tcpdump -i any` duplicates and out-of-order segments.** Each segment is seen once per interface it crosses. A large ClientHello (Go 1.26's X25519MLKEM768 key share) spans segments. `TestL73ConnsReassemblesOutOfOrderDuplicates` and `TestL73StreamDropsOverlapsAndStopsAtAGap` cover this (Task 4).
4. **A half-applied device configuration.**
   - On Junos, `apply` rolls back a refused commit.
   - IOS XE, NX-OS and EOS (as typed here) have no commit. Every such change is the previous cell's undo followed by this cell's lines. On every path, a `t.Fatal` included, a cleanup runs the full undo *leniently*, so a line refused because it was never applied does not stop the rest. `TestL73CiscoUndoIsTheStartupConfig` pins the lines (Task 6a).
5. **A Cisco dialogue the harness does not know.** An enrolment question with no step waits 180s, then fails INVALID with the scrubbed screen. It never answers a question it does not recognize. `TestL73NextAnswer` covers ordering and the prompt (Task 6a).
6. **Junos help markers** (`+ apply-groups`, `> tls`). A container option must read as present. `TestL71SaysReadsJunosMarkedOptions` covers this (Task 7).
7. **The wrong daemon measured.** Two things would make a cell measure the wrong daemon: port49d restarted on the standing config after the device is configured, or a device that remembers an earlier refusal. `startOn`, `ready`, `revive` and `reached` are the defences (ruling items 6 and 7). `TestL73DaemonRunsOnlyTheCellsConfig` pins them (Task 8).

### Task 1: The image builds: the gate, the recipes, and the router

No code. Each image is built **just before its run**: the router here, the others in their run tasks (12, 13, 15, 16), from the recipes below. cEOS needs no build: Task 14 checks that `ceos:4.36.3M` is present. The host is AMD (Ryzen 7 8745HS), so the vJunos CPU check is patched by hand: `ghcr.io/birbnetworks/vjunos-amd-enable` is not pullable. Plan 1 Task 15 used the same patch for 23.4.

**Files:**
- Modify: `tests/lab/README.md` (Setup step 3's image list and expected tags)

**The disk gate** (run before every build; `PEAK_M` is the recipe's estimate):
```sh
avail=$(df -BM --output=avail / | tail -1 | tr -dc 0-9)
echo "need $(( 67584 + PEAK_M ))M, have ${avail}M"
```
If `have < need`, free space in this order (ruling 2), re-checking after each step and stopping as soon as the gate passes:

1. `go clean -cache`. It is regenerable.
2. `docker rmi vrnetlab/cisco_c8000v:controller-17.13.01a`. Run `docker image inspect` on it first, and check the tag is exactly that one.
3. This plan's own earlier L73 images, oldest first, one at a time. Their results are committed, and the README says how to rebuild them. Never remove `ceos:4.36.3M` before Task 14 has run.

Record every step taken in the task report. Never remove any other image. If the gate still fails after step 3, **do not skip the build**: stop and report the numbers to the team lead. That applies to the switch too (ruling 2).

| Build | Source in `/mnt/images/` | Tag | `PEAK_M` (estimate) |
|---|---|---|---|
| router | `vJunos-router-26.2R1.7.qcow2` | `vrnetlab/juniper_vjunos-router:26.2R1.7` | 12800 |
| C8000V | `c8000v-universalk9_8G_serial.17.18.02.qcow2` | `vrnetlab/cisco_c8000v:17.18.02` | 9500 |
| N9Kv | `nexus9300v64.10.6.2.F.qcow2` | `vrnetlab/cisco_n9kv:9300v64-10.6.2.F` | 9700 |
| IOL-XE | `iol-xe-17-18-02.tar.gz` | `vrnetlab/cisco_iol:17.18.02` | 600 |
| XRd | `ios-xrd-control-plane-26-1-1.tar.gz` | `port49lab/xrd-control-plane:26.1.1` | 1500 |
| switch | `vJunos-switch-26.2R1.7.qcow2` | `vrnetlab/juniper_vjunos-switch:26.2R1.7` | 14700 |

How the estimates are made: each is the qcow2 copy plus the image, scaled from this host's earlier builds. vJunos 23.4 grew about 2.3× its qcow2, c8000v 17.13 about 3.0×, and n9kv 10.4.2.F about 2.4×. C8000V gets 2000M more for its install container's commit. After each build, record the real Δ (Avail before − after) in the README. A later build of the same family uses the measured Δ plus its copy in place of the estimate.

**Under the lab lock.** A build boots VMs (C8000V's install step) and fills the disk, so it holds the lab's lock as a run does, and never waits on or breaks someone else's. Run each recipe as one shell invocation that starts with:
```sh
exec 9>>/tmp/port49-lab.lock && flock -n 9 || { echo "the lab is occupied: $(cat /tmp/port49-lab.lock)"; exit 1; }
```
`>>` and not `>`: the file names its holder, and opening it must not erase that. The lock drops when the shell exits. If it is occupied, report and wait for the holder (plan 11a's lab tasks take the same lock).

**Recipe R (vJunos router or switch).** `D` is `vjunosrouter` or `vjunosswitch`, and `F` is the qcow2's file name:
```sh
cd ~/git/vrnetlab/juniper/$D
cp /mnt/images/$F . && sha256sum $F /mnt/images/$F        # the sums must match; record one
gf() { docker run --rm --device /dev/kvm -e LIBGUESTFS_DEBUG=0 -v "$PWD":/work -w /work cmattoon/guestfish "$@"; }
gf -a $F run : list-filesystems                         # expect /dev/sda2 among them (guestfish needs run first)
gf -a $F -m /dev/sda2 download /home/pfe/junos/start-junos.sh /work/start-junos.sh
grep -c 'CPU_FLAG=' start-junos.sh                         # expect exactly 1; anything else: STOP and report
sed -i 's@CPU_FLAG=.*@CPU_FLAG=$(cat /proc/cpuinfo | grep -ci "vmx\\|svm")@' start-junos.sh
grep -n 'CPU_FLAG=' start-junos.sh                         # expect: CPU_FLAG=$(cat /proc/cpuinfo | grep -ci "vmx\|svm")
gf -a $F -m /dev/sda2 upload /work/start-junos.sh /home/pfe/junos/start-junos.sh
rm start-junos.sh
sg docker -c "make IMAGE=$F docker-build" && rm -f $F
```
zsh does not word-split a variable, which is why `gf` is a function. If `download` fails, run `gf -a $F -m /dev/sda2 ls /home/pfe/junos`. If `start-junos.sh` is not there, stop and report the listing. Do not guess another path.

**Recipe C (C8000V).** The explicit `IMAGE=` keeps the 17.13 qcow2, if one is present, out of the build. `MODE=autonomous` boots the VM once for its install step.
```sh
cd ~/git/vrnetlab/cisco/c8000v
cp /mnt/images/c8000v-universalk9_8G_serial.17.18.02.qcow2 . && sha256sum c8000v-universalk9_8G_serial.17.18.02.qcow2
sg docker -c "make IMAGE=c8000v-universalk9_8G_serial.17.18.02.qcow2 MODE=autonomous docker-build"
rm -f c8000v-universalk9_8G_serial.17.18.02.qcow2
```

**Recipe N (N9Kv).** The Makefile derives the tag from `n9kv-<version>.qcow2`, so the copy is renamed. The 10.4.2.F leftover in the directory goes first, and only after `cmp` proves it is a copy:
```sh
cd ~/git/vrnetlab/cisco/n9kv
cmp n9kv-9300v64-10.4.2.F.qcow2 /mnt/images/nexus9300v64.10.4.2.F.qcow2 && rm n9kv-9300v64-10.4.2.F.qcow2   # cmp must print nothing; if it differs, STOP and report
cp /mnt/images/nexus9300v64.10.6.2.F.qcow2 n9kv-9300v64-10.6.2.F.qcow2 && sha256sum n9kv-9300v64-10.6.2.F.qcow2
sg docker -c "make IMAGE=n9kv-9300v64-10.6.2.F.qcow2 docker-build"
rm -f n9kv-9300v64-10.6.2.F.qcow2
```

**Recipe I (IOL-XE).** The Makefile peels the IOL binary out of CML's container archive.
```sh
cd ~/git/vrnetlab/cisco/iol
cp /mnt/images/iol-xe-17-18-02.tar.gz . && sha256sum iol-xe-17-18-02.tar.gz
sg docker -c "make IMAGE=iol-xe-17-18-02.tar.gz docker-build"
rm -f iol-xe-17-18-02.tar.gz
```

**Recipe X (XRd).**
```sh
sha256sum /mnt/images/ios-xrd-control-plane-26-1-1.tar.gz
sg docker -c "docker load -i /mnt/images/ios-xrd-control-plane-26-1-1.tar.gz"     # record the "Loaded image:" line
sg docker -c "docker tag <loaded image> port49lab/xrd-control-plane:26.1.1"
```

After every recipe, run `docker image inspect <tag> --format '{{.Id}} {{.Size}}'` and `df -BM / | tail -1`, and record the ID, the size and the Δ. If `docker system df` shows build cache, run `docker builder prune -f`.

After `var platforms`, add:
```go
// optInPlatforms are the rows a run deploys only when PORT49_LAB_PLATFORMS
// names them.
var optInPlatforms = map[string]bool{"vjunos26": true, "iosxe1718": true, "nxos106": true, "ceos436": true,
	"iolxe1718": true, "xrd261": true, "vjunosswitch26": true}

// helpOnlyPlatforms come up with a break-glass account and no TACACS+: up()
// starts no port49d for them, and TestL73 asks their CLI help only.
var helpOnlyPlatforms = map[string]bool{"iolxe1718": true, "xrd261": true}

// defaultPlatforms is the selection when PORT49_LAB_PLATFORMS is empty.
func defaultPlatforms(ps []platform) []platform {
	var out []platform
	for _, p := range ps {
		if !optInPlatforms[p.sel] {
			out = append(out, p)
		}
	}
	return out
}
```

### Task 3: The lab's single-occupant lock

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/lab/occupancy.go`, `tests/lab/occupancy_unit_test.go`
- Modify: `tests/lab/harness_test.go` (`TestMain` releases the lock after teardown)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `const labLockPath = "/tmp/port49-lab.lock"`, `func labOccupy(path, who string) (release func(), err error)`, `func labOccupyForRun(path, who string) error` and `func labRelease()`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** `tests/lab/occupancy_unit_test.go`:
```go
package lab

import (
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// A second taker is refused while the first holds the lock, is told who
// holds it, and gets it once the first lets go.
func TestLabOccupyIsExclusive(t *testing.T) {
	p := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "lab.lock")
	release, err := labOccupy(p, "first run")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if _, err := labOccupy(p, "second run"); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "first run") {
		t.Fatalf("a second taker got %v, want a refusal naming the holder", err)
	}
	release()
	again, err := labOccupy(p, "second run")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("after release: %v", err)
	}
	again()
}

// A run's lock outlives the test that took it: it is let go only by
// labRelease, which TestMain calls after teardown. Taking it twice in one
// process is a no-op, so every subtest of a run can ask.
func TestLabOccupyForRunHoldsUntilReleased(t *testing.T) {
	p := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "lab.lock")
	if err := labOccupyForRun(p, "this run"); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	t.Cleanup(labRelease)
	if err := labOccupyForRun(p, "this run again"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("a second take in the same run: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := labOccupy(p, "another run"); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "this run") {
		t.Fatalf("another taker got %v, want a refusal naming this run", err)
	}
	labRelease()
	again, err := labOccupy(p, "another run")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("after labRelease: %v", err)
	}
	again()
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it and see it fail.** Run: `go test ./tests/lab/ -run '^TestLabOccupy' -count=1`. Expected: `undefined: labOccupy` (and `labOccupyForRun`, `labRelease`).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `tests/lab/occupancy.go`:
```go
package lab

import (
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"strings"
	"syscall"
)

// labLockPath is the lab's single-occupant lock: one containerlab topology,
// one port49d on the bridge's port 49 and 300. A run that deploys a device
// takes it first and holds it to the end; a second run is refused at once,
// told who holds it, rather than tearing down the first run's device.
const labLockPath = "/tmp/port49-lab.lock"

// labOccupy takes the exclusive, non-blocking lock on path and writes who
// into it. release lets it go; the lock also dies with the process.
func labOccupy(path, who string) (release func(), err error) {
	f, err := os.OpenFile(path, os.O_CREATE|os.O_RDWR, 0o600)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if err := syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX|syscall.LOCK_NB); err != nil {
		holder, _ := os.ReadFile(path)
		f.Close()
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("the lab is occupied: %s holds %s (%v); wait for it, never kill it",
			strings.TrimSpace(string(holder)), path, err)
	}
	if err := f.Truncate(0); err == nil {
		_, _ = f.WriteAt([]byte(who+"\n"), 0)
	}
	return func() { f.Close() }, nil
}

// labHeld is the run's lock. A package variable keeps the file reachable:
// an unreachable *os.File is closed by its finalizer, and the flock with it.
var labHeld func()

// labOccupyForRun takes the lock for the rest of the process. TestMain lets
// it go after teardown (labRelease), so the next run never deploys beside a
// topology that containerlab is still destroying; a signal's exit lets it go
// with the process.
func labOccupyForRun(path, who string) error {
	if labHeld != nil {
		return nil
	}
	release, err := labOccupy(path, who)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	labHeld = release
	return nil
}

// labRelease lets go of the run's lock, if this process holds it.
func labRelease() {
	if labHeld != nil {
		labHeld()
		labHeld = nil
	}
}
```

### Task 4: A capture's TLS connections, summarized with no session bytes

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/lab/l73_tlswire.go`, `tests/lab/l73_tlswire_unit_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `labpcap.Parse`, `labpcap.FlowOf`, `(*labpcap.File).Payload`, `(*labpcap.File).TimeOf`, `labpcap.LinkEthernet`.
- Produces:
  - `type l73Hello`, `type l73ServerHello`, `type l73Alert`, `type l73Plain`, `type l73Conn` (JSON shapes below).
  - `func l73Conns(raw []byte, device string) ([]l73Conn, error)`
  - `func l73SummaryLeaks(s string) []string`
  - `func l73FirstPort(conns []l73Conn, host string) (uint16, bool)`
  - `func l73Window(conns []l73Conn, host string, from, to time.Time) []l73Conn`
  - `func l73Describe(conns []l73Conn, from time.Time) string`
  - `func l73Host(server string) string`, `func l73PortOf(server string) uint16`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** `tests/lab/l73_tlswire_unit_test.go`:
```go
package lab

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/tls"
	"encoding/binary"
	"encoding/hex"
	"encoding/json"
	"io"
	"maps"
	"net"
	"reflect"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/blanketfish/port49/internal/testpki"
	"github.com/blanketfish/port49/tests/lab/labpcap"
)

const (
	l73TDevice = "172.100.100.21"
	l73TServer = "172.100.100.1"
)

type l73FakeSeg struct {
	fromDevice bool
	flags      byte
	seq        uint32
	payload    []byte
	at         time.Time
}

// l73FakePcap writes segs as an Ethernet savefile between the device and the
// server, as tcpdump would.
func l73FakePcap(devPort, srvPort uint16, segs []l73FakeSeg) []byte {
	var b bytes.Buffer
	hdr := make([]byte, 24)
	binary.LittleEndian.PutUint32(hdr[0:], 0xa1b2c3d4)
	binary.LittleEndian.PutUint16(hdr[4:], 2)
	binary.LittleEndian.PutUint16(hdr[6:], 4)
	binary.LittleEndian.PutUint32(hdr[16:], 262144)
	binary.LittleEndian.PutUint32(hdr[20:], uint32(labpcap.LinkEthernet))
	b.Write(hdr)
	for _, s := range segs {
		src, dst, sp, dp := l73TDevice, l73TServer, devPort, srvPort
		if !s.fromDevice {
			src, dst, sp, dp = l73TServer, l73TDevice, srvPort, devPort
		}
		pkt := make([]byte, 14+20+20+len(s.payload))
		binary.BigEndian.PutUint16(pkt[12:], 0x0800)
		ip := pkt[14:]
		ip[0] = 0x45
		binary.BigEndian.PutUint16(ip[2:], uint16(40+len(s.payload)))
		ip[8], ip[9] = 64, 6
		copy(ip[12:16], net.ParseIP(src).To4())
		copy(ip[16:20], net.ParseIP(dst).To4())
		tcp := ip[20:]
		binary.BigEndian.PutUint16(tcp[0:], sp)
		binary.BigEndian.PutUint16(tcp[2:], dp)
		binary.BigEndian.PutUint32(tcp[4:], s.seq)
		tcp[12] = 5 << 4
		tcp[13] = s.flags
		copy(tcp[20:], s.payload)
		rec := make([]byte, 16)
		binary.LittleEndian.PutUint32(rec[0:], uint32(s.at.Unix()))
		binary.LittleEndian.PutUint32(rec[4:], uint32(s.at.Nanosecond()/1000))
		binary.LittleEndian.PutUint32(rec[8:], uint32(len(pkt)))
		binary.LittleEndian.PutUint32(rec[12:], uint32(len(pkt)))
		b.Write(rec)
		b.Write(pkt)
	}
	return b.Bytes()
}

const (
	tcpFIN = 0x01
	tcpSYN = 0x02
	tcpRST = 0x04
	tcpPSH = 0x08
	tcpACK = 0x10
)

// l73Conversation is a whole connection: handshake, up in 1000-byte
// segments, down likewise, the device's FIN. reverse sends the device's
// segments last-first. Every segment appears twice, as -i any shows it.
func l73Conversation(up, down []byte, at time.Time, reverse bool) []l73FakeSeg {
	segs := []l73FakeSeg{{true, tcpSYN, 1000, nil, at}, {false, tcpSYN | tcpACK, 5000, nil, at}}
	var ups []l73FakeSeg
	seq := uint32(1001)
	for i := 0; i < len(up); i += 1000 {
		j := min(i+1000, len(up))
		ups = append(ups, l73FakeSeg{true, tcpACK | tcpPSH, seq, up[i:j], at})
		seq += uint32(j - i)
	}
	if reverse {
		for i, j := 0, len(ups)-1; i < j; i, j = i+1, j-1 {
			ups[i], ups[j] = ups[j], ups[i]
		}
	}
	segs = append(segs, ups...)
	sseq := uint32(5001)
	for i := 0; i < len(down); i += 1000 {
		j := min(i+1000, len(down))
		segs = append(segs, l73FakeSeg{false, tcpACK | tcpPSH, sseq, down[i:j], at})
		sseq += uint32(j - i)
	}
	segs = append(segs, l73FakeSeg{true, tcpFIN | tcpACK, seq, nil, at.Add(time.Second)})
	var twice []l73FakeSeg
	for _, s := range segs {
		twice = append(twice, s, s)
	}
	return twice
}

type l73Tee struct {
	net.Conn
	w *bytes.Buffer
}

func (c *l73Tee) Write(p []byte) (int, error) { c.w.Write(p); return c.Conn.Write(p) }

// l73RealHandshake runs crypto/tls over loopback TCP and returns the bytes
// each side wrote: real ClientHello and ServerHello bytes to parse.
func l73RealHandshake(t *testing.T, srv, cli *tls.Config) (up, down []byte) {
	t.Helper()
	ln, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	defer ln.Close()
	var downBuf bytes.Buffer
	done := make(chan error, 1)
	go func() {
		raw, err := ln.Accept()
		if err != nil {
			done <- err
			return
		}
		s := tls.Server(&l73Tee{Conn: raw, w: &downBuf}, srv)
		if err := s.Handshake(); err != nil {
			raw.Close()
			done <- err
			return
		}
		_, err = s.Write([]byte("x"))
		s.Close()
		done <- err
	}()
	raw, err := net.Dial("tcp", ln.Addr().String())
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var upBuf bytes.Buffer
	c := tls.Client(&l73Tee{Conn: raw, w: &upBuf}, cli)
	if err := c.Handshake(); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if _, err := io.ReadFull(c, make([]byte, 1)); err != nil { // takes the NewSessionTicket with it
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	c.Close()
	if err := <-done; err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return upBuf.Bytes(), downBuf.Bytes()
}

func l73TestConfigs(t *testing.T) (*tls.Config, *tls.Config) {
	t.Helper()
	now := time.Now()
	ca, err := testpki.NewCA("L73 unit CA", now.Add(-time.Hour), now.Add(time.Hour))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	leaf, err := ca.Issue(testpki.Leaf{DNSNames: []string{"tacacs.l73.example"},
		NotBefore: now.Add(-time.Minute), NotAfter: now.Add(time.Hour)})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return &tls.Config{Certificates: []tls.Certificate{leaf}, MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS13},
		&tls.Config{RootCAs: ca.Pool(), ServerName: "tacacs.l73.example", MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS13,
			ClientSessionCache: tls.NewLRUClientSessionCache(4)}
}

func TestL73ConnsReadsAFullHandshake(t *testing.T) {
	srv, cli := l73TestConfigs(t)
	up, down := l73RealHandshake(t, srv, cli)
	at := time.Unix(1_800_000_000, 0)
	conns, err := l73Conns(l73FakePcap(40001, 300, l73Conversation(up, down, at, false)), l73TDevice)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if len(conns) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("%d connections, want 1: %+v", len(conns), conns)
	}
	c := conns[0]
	if c.Server != "172.100.100.1:300" || c.Answer != "syn-ack" || c.End != "device-fin" || c.Hello == nil || c.ServerHello == nil {
		t.Fatalf("%+v", c)
	}
	if !c.Hello.HasSNI || c.Hello.SNI != "tacacs.l73.example" || c.Hello.PSKIdentities != 0 {
		t.Errorf("hello %+v", c.Hello)
	}
	if c.ServerHello.Version != tls.VersionTLS13 || tls.CipherSuiteName(c.ServerHello.Suite) == "" || c.ServerHello.PSKSelected {
		t.Errorf("server hello %+v", c.ServerHello)
	}
	if c.DeviceRecords == 0 || c.ServerRecords == 0 {
		t.Errorf("records %d %d", c.DeviceRecords, c.ServerRecords)
	}
}

// Go 1.26's ClientHello carries an X25519MLKEM768 share and spans segments;
// -i any shows each twice; the device's segments arrive last-first.
func TestL73ConnsReassemblesOutOfOrderDuplicates(t *testing.T) {
	srv, cli := l73TestConfigs(t)
	up, down := l73RealHandshake(t, srv, cli)
	if len(up) <= 1000 {
		t.Skip("this ClientHello fits one segment; nothing to reassemble")
	}
	conns, err := l73Conns(l73FakePcap(40002, 300, l73Conversation(up, down, time.Unix(1_800_000_000, 0), true)), l73TDevice)
	if err != nil || len(conns) != 1 || conns[0].Hello == nil || len(conns[0].Hello.Groups) == 0 {
		t.Fatalf("%+v %v", conns, err)
	}
}

// l73Stream is one direction in sequence order: an exact duplicate and a
// partial overlap add nothing, an out-of-order segment lands in place, and a
// gap ends the stream (what follows one was not captured).
func TestL73StreamDropsOverlapsAndStopsAtAGap(t *testing.T) {
	segs := []l73Seg{
		{seq: 1006, data: []byte("ghij")}, // out of order
		{seq: 1000, data: []byte("abcdef")},
		{seq: 1000, data: []byte("abcdef")}, // an exact duplicate
		{seq: 1004, data: []byte("efgh")},   // a partial overlap
		{seq: 1020, data: []byte("zz")},     // past a gap
	}
	if got := string(l73Stream(segs)); got != "abcdefghij" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q, want %q", got, "abcdefghij")
	}
}

// The summary's types leave no room for session material. Every field is
// listed here with its type, and none is a byte string: a field that could
// carry a random, a legacy session id, a key share, a PSK identity, a
// binder or a ticket fails this test, and a new field fails it until it is
// reviewed and listed.
func TestL73SummaryTypesHaveNoRoomForSessionBytes(t *testing.T) {
	want := map[string]string{
		"l73Conn.Server": "string", "l73Conn.DevicePort": "uint16", "l73Conn.SYNAt": "time.Time",
		"l73Conn.Answer": "string", "l73Conn.Hello": "*lab.l73Hello", "l73Conn.ServerHello": "*lab.l73ServerHello",
		"l73Conn.Alerts": "[]lab.l73Alert", "l73Conn.DeviceRecords": "int", "l73Conn.ServerRecords": "int",
		"l73Conn.Plain": "[]lab.l73Plain", "l73Conn.End": "string", "l73Conn.EndAt": "time.Time",
		"l73Hello.LegacyVersion": "uint16", "l73Hello.Versions": "[]uint16", "l73Hello.Suites": "[]uint16",
		"l73Hello.HasSNI": "bool", "l73Hello.SNI": "string", "l73Hello.Groups": "[]uint16",
		"l73Hello.SigAlgs": "[]uint16", "l73Hello.ALPN": "[]string", "l73Hello.PSKIdentities": "int",
		"l73Hello.PSKModes": "[]int", "l73Hello.EarlyData": "bool", "l73Hello.Extensions": "[]uint16",
		"l73ServerHello.Version": "uint16", "l73ServerHello.Suite": "uint16",
		"l73ServerHello.PSKSelected": "bool", "l73ServerHello.HelloRetry": "bool",
		"l73Alert.Level": "uint8", "l73Alert.Description": "uint8", "l73Alert.FromDevice": "bool",
		"l73Plain.Type": "uint8", "l73Plain.SeqNo": "uint8", "l73Plain.Flags": "uint8", "l73Plain.Length": "uint32",
	}
	got := map[string]string{}
	pkg := reflect.TypeOf(l73Conn{}).PkgPath()
	seen := map[reflect.Type]bool{}
	var walk func(reflect.Type)
	walk = func(rt reflect.Type) {
		for rt.Kind() == reflect.Pointer || rt.Kind() == reflect.Slice {
			rt = rt.Elem()
		}
		if rt.Kind() != reflect.Struct || rt.PkgPath() != pkg || seen[rt] {
			return
		}
		seen[rt] = true
		for i := range rt.NumField() {
			f := rt.Field(i)
			got[rt.Name()+"."+f.Name] = f.Type.String()
			walk(f.Type)
		}
	}
	walk(reflect.TypeOf(l73Conn{}))
	if !maps.Equal(got, want) {
		t.Fatalf("the summary's fields changed:\n got %v\nwant %v", got, want)
	}
}

// A resumption shows as a PSK identity offered and selected, and nothing of
// the identity, the binder, the randoms or the legacy session id reaches
// the summary.
func TestL73ResumptionSummaryHoldsNoSessionBytes(t *testing.T) {
	srv, cli := l73TestConfigs(t)
	l73RealHandshake(t, srv, cli)
	up, down := l73RealHandshake(t, srv, cli)
	conns, err := l73Conns(l73FakePcap(40003, 300, l73Conversation(up, down, time.Unix(1_800_000_000, 0), false)), l73TDevice)
	if err != nil || len(conns) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("%+v %v", conns, err)
	}
	if conns[0].Hello.PSKIdentities != 1 || !conns[0].ServerHello.PSKSelected {
		t.Fatalf("not seen as a resumption: %+v %+v", conns[0].Hello, conns[0].ServerHello)
	}
	js, err := json.Marshal(conns)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if hits := l73SummaryLeaks(string(js)); len(hits) > 0 {
		t.Fatalf("summary holds %v: %s", hits, js)
	}
	// record header 5, handshake header 4, legacy_version 2, random 32, then
	// the session id's length byte: none of the id or the random survives.
	sid := up[5+4+2+32+1 : 5+4+2+32+1+int(up[5+4+2+32])]
	random := up[5+4+2 : 5+4+2+32]
	for _, b := range [][]byte{sid, random} {
		if len(b) > 0 && strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(string(js)), hex.EncodeToString(b)[:16]) {
			t.Fatalf("summary holds session bytes: %s", js)
		}
	}
}

func TestL73ConnsRefusedSYN(t *testing.T) {
	at := time.Unix(1_800_000_000, 0)
	segs := []l73FakeSeg{{true, tcpSYN, 7, nil, at}, {false, tcpRST | tcpACK, 0, nil, at.Add(time.Millisecond)}}
	conns, err := l73Conns(l73FakePcap(40004, 6049, segs), l73TDevice)
	if err != nil || len(conns) != 1 || conns[0].Answer != "rst" || conns[0].End != "server-rst" || conns[0].Hello != nil {
		t.Fatalf("%+v %v", conns, err)
	}
	if p, ok := l73FirstPort(conns, l73TServer); !ok || p != 6049 {
		t.Fatalf("first port %d %v", p, ok)
	}
}

// Plain TACACS+ on 49 is summarized by its headers alone: the body (a
// password under the shared key) never reaches the summary.
func TestL73ConnsPlainTACACSHeadersOnly(t *testing.T) {
	body := []byte("SECRETBODY")
	hdr := []byte{0xc1, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 7, 0, 0, 0, byte(len(body))}
	at := time.Unix(1_800_000_000, 0)
	segs := []l73FakeSeg{{true, tcpSYN, 1, nil, at}, {false, tcpSYN | tcpACK, 9, nil, at},
		{true, tcpACK | tcpPSH, 2, append(hdr, body...), at}}
	conns, err := l73Conns(l73FakePcap(40005, 49, segs), l73TDevice)
	if err != nil || len(conns) != 1 || len(conns[0].Plain) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("%+v %v", conns, err)
	}
	if p := conns[0].Plain[0]; p.Type != 1 || p.SeqNo != 1 || p.Flags != 0 || p.Length != uint32(len(body)) {
		t.Fatalf("%+v", p)
	}
	js, _ := json.Marshal(conns)
	if strings.Contains(string(js), "SECRETBODY") {
		t.Fatal("the body reached the summary")
	}
}

func TestL73ConnsIgnoresSSHAndNETCONF(t *testing.T) {
	at := time.Unix(1_800_000_000, 0)
	for _, port := range []uint16{22, 830} {
		conns, err := l73Conns(l73FakePcap(40006, port, []l73FakeSeg{{true, tcpSYN, 1, nil, at}}), l73TDevice)
		if err != nil || len(conns) != 0 {
			t.Fatalf("port %d: %+v %v", port, conns, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestL73FirstPortNeedsAConnection(t *testing.T) {
	if p, ok := l73FirstPort(nil, l73TServer); ok || p != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("%d %v", p, ok)
	}
	if got := l73Describe(nil, time.Now()); got != "the device opened no connection" {
		t.Fatal(got)
	}
}

func TestL73WindowAndDescribe(t *testing.T) {
	t0 := time.Unix(1_800_000_000, 0)
	conns := []l73Conn{
		{Server: "172.100.100.1:300", SYNAt: t0.Add(time.Second), Answer: "syn-ack", End: "server-rst", EndAt: t0.Add(2 * time.Second)},
		{Server: "172.100.100.2:300", SYNAt: t0.Add(3 * time.Second), Answer: "rst", End: "server-rst", EndAt: t0.Add(3 * time.Second)},
	}
	if w := l73Window(conns, "172.100.100.1", t0, t0.Add(10*time.Second)); len(w) != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("%+v", w)
	}
	want := "+1.00s to 172.100.100.1:300: syn-ack, ended server-rst at +2.00s; +3.00s to 172.100.100.2:300: rst, ended server-rst at +3.00s"
	if got := l73Describe(conns, t0); got != want {
		t.Fatalf("got  %s\nwant %s", got, want)
	}
}

func TestL73SummaryLeaksFlagsLongRuns(t *testing.T) {
	if len(l73SummaryLeaks(`{"server":"172.100.100.1:300","syn_at":"2026-10-17T03:15:29.140123Z"}`)) != 0 {
		t.Fatal("an ordinary summary flagged")
	}
	if len(l73SummaryLeaks(`{"x":"`+strings.Repeat("ab", 16)+`"}`)) == 0 {
		t.Fatal("32 hex characters not flagged")
	}
	if len(l73SummaryLeaks(`{"x":"`+strings.Repeat("QUJD", 10)+`"}`)) == 0 {
		t.Fatal("40 base64 characters not flagged")
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run them and see them fail.** Run: `go test ./tests/lab/ -run '^TestL73' -count=1`. Expected: `undefined: l73Conns` (and the other names).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `tests/lab/l73_tlswire.go`:
```go
package lab

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/tls"
	"encoding/binary"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net"
	"regexp"
	"sort"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/blanketfish/port49/tests/lab/labpcap"
)

// L73's captures are TLS. What is in the clear is the ClientHello, the
// ServerHello and any alert sent before the keys change; everything after is
// ciphertext. The committed evidence is a summary of each connection made
// from those, and its types have no field for session material: no random,
// no legacy session id, no key share, no PSK identity or binder. Counts and
// names stand for those. The raw capture stays in tests/lab/captures/
// (git-ignored).

// l73Hello is what a device's ClientHello says, minus every session byte.
type l73Hello struct {
	LegacyVersion uint16   `json:"legacy_version"`
	Versions      []uint16 `json:"supported_versions,omitempty"`
	Suites        []uint16 `json:"cipher_suites"`
	HasSNI        bool     `json:"has_sni"`
	SNI           string   `json:"sni,omitempty"`
	Groups        []uint16 `json:"supported_groups,omitempty"`
	SigAlgs       []uint16 `json:"signature_algorithms,omitempty"`
	ALPN          []string `json:"alpn,omitempty"`
	PSKIdentities int      `json:"psk_identities"`
	PSKModes      []int    `json:"psk_key_exchange_modes,omitempty"`
	EarlyData     bool     `json:"early_data"`
	Extensions    []uint16 `json:"extensions"`
}

// l73ServerHello is port49's answer: the version and suite it chose, and
// whether it accepted a PSK (a resumption).
type l73ServerHello struct {
	Version     uint16 `json:"selected_version"`
	Suite       uint16 `json:"cipher_suite"`
	PSKSelected bool   `json:"psk_selected"`
	HelloRetry  bool   `json:"hello_retry_request"`
}

// l73Alert is an alert sent in the clear (before the keys change).
type l73Alert struct {
	Level       uint8 `json:"level"`
	Description uint8 `json:"description"`
	FromDevice  bool  `json:"from_device"`
}

// l73Plain is one RFC 8907 header the device sent, without its body.
type l73Plain struct {
	Type   uint8  `json:"type"`
	SeqNo  uint8  `json:"seq_no"`
	Flags  uint8  `json:"flags"`
	Length uint32 `json:"length"`
}

// l73Conn is one TCP connection the device opened.
type l73Conn struct {
	Server        string          `json:"server"`
	DevicePort    uint16          `json:"device_port"`
	SYNAt         time.Time       `json:"syn_at"`
	Answer        string          `json:"answer"` // syn-ack, rst, none
	Hello         *l73Hello       `json:"client_hello,omitempty"`
	ServerHello   *l73ServerHello `json:"server_hello,omitempty"`
	Alerts        []l73Alert      `json:"plaintext_alerts,omitempty"`
	DeviceRecords int             `json:"device_tls_records"`
	ServerRecords int             `json:"server_tls_records"`
	// Byte counts and the device's first FIN and RST: a count, never the
	// bytes. End keeps only the last event; these say who closed first.
	DevicePayloadBytes int        `json:"device_payload_bytes"`
	ServerPayloadBytes int        `json:"server_payload_bytes"`
	DeviceFINAt        time.Time  `json:"device_fin_at,omitzero"`
	DeviceRSTAt        time.Time  `json:"device_rst_at,omitzero"`
	Plain         []l73Plain      `json:"plain_tacacs_from_device,omitempty"`
	End           string          `json:"end"` // device-fin, server-fin, device-rst, server-rst, open
	EndAt         time.Time       `json:"end_at"`
}

type l73Seg struct {
	seq  uint32
	data []byte
}

// l73Conns reads a savefile for every TCP connection the device opened,
// SSH (22) and NETCONF (830) aside, in the order their SYNs were captured.
// tcpdump -i any sees a segment on each interface it crosses; the second
// sighting is dropped.
func l73Conns(raw []byte, device string) ([]l73Conn, error) {
	f, err := labpcap.Parse(raw)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	type key struct {
		port   uint16
		server string
	}
	type state struct {
		c        *l73Conn
		up, down []l73Seg
		seen     map[[4]uint32]bool
	}
	conns := map[key]*state{}
	var order []key
	for i, r := range f.Records {
		fl, ok := labpcap.FlowOf(f.Link, f.Bytes(r))
		if !ok {
			continue
		}
		fromDevice := fl.Src.String() == device
		var k key
		var serverPort uint16
		switch {
		case fromDevice:
			k, serverPort = key{fl.SrcPort, net.JoinHostPort(fl.Dst.String(), strconv.Itoa(int(fl.DstPort)))}, fl.DstPort
		case fl.Dst.String() == device:
			k, serverPort = key{fl.DstPort, net.JoinHostPort(fl.Src.String(), strconv.Itoa(int(fl.SrcPort)))}, fl.SrcPort
		default:
			continue
		}
		if serverPort == 22 || serverPort == 830 {
			continue
		}
		st := conns[k]
		if st == nil {
			if !(fromDevice && fl.SYN()) {
				continue // a connection is counted from its SYN
			}
			st = &state{c: &l73Conn{Server: k.server, DevicePort: k.port, SYNAt: f.TimeOf(i), Answer: "none", End: "open"},
				seen: map[[4]uint32]bool{}}
			conns[k] = st
			order = append(order, k)
		}
		dir := uint32(0)
		if !fromDevice {
			dir = 1
		}
		id := [4]uint32{dir, fl.Seq, uint32(fl.PayloadLen), uint32(fl.Flags)}
		if st.seen[id] {
			continue
		}
		st.seen[id] = true
		const fin, syn, rst, ack = 0x01, 0x02, 0x04, 0x10
		if !fromDevice && st.c.Answer == "none" {
			switch {
			case fl.Flags&syn != 0 && fl.Flags&ack != 0:
				st.c.Answer = "syn-ack"
			case fl.Flags&rst != 0:
				st.c.Answer = "rst"
			}
		}
		if fromDevice {
			st.c.DevicePayloadBytes += fl.PayloadLen
			if fl.Flags&fin != 0 && st.c.DeviceFINAt.IsZero() {
				st.c.DeviceFINAt = f.TimeOf(i)
			}
			if fl.Flags&rst != 0 && st.c.DeviceRSTAt.IsZero() {
				st.c.DeviceRSTAt = f.TimeOf(i)
			}
		} else {
			st.c.ServerPayloadBytes += fl.PayloadLen
		}
		if fl.Flags&(rst|fin) != 0 && st.c.End == "open" {
			who, kind := "device", "fin"
			if !fromDevice {
				who = "server"
			}
			if fl.Flags&rst != 0 {
				kind = "rst"
			}
			st.c.End, st.c.EndAt = who+"-"+kind, f.TimeOf(i)
		}
		if fl.PayloadLen > 0 {
			p := append([]byte(nil), f.Payload(r, fl)...)
			if fromDevice {
				st.up = append(st.up, l73Seg{fl.Seq, p})
			} else {
				st.down = append(st.down, l73Seg{fl.Seq, p})
			}
		}
	}
	out := make([]l73Conn, 0, len(order))
	for _, k := range order {
		st := conns[k]
		c := st.c
		up, down := l73Stream(st.up), l73Stream(st.down)
		switch {
		case l73IsTLS(up):
			for _, r := range l73Records(up) {
				c.DeviceRecords++
				if r.typ == 21 && len(r.body) == 2 {
					c.Alerts = append(c.Alerts, l73Alert{r.body[0], r.body[1], true})
				}
			}
			for _, r := range l73Records(down) {
				c.ServerRecords++
				if r.typ == 21 && len(r.body) == 2 {
					c.Alerts = append(c.Alerts, l73Alert{r.body[0], r.body[1], false})
				}
			}
			if m, ok := l73FirstHandshake(up, 1); ok {
				h, err := l73ParseClientHello(m)
				if err != nil {
					return nil, fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", c.Server, err)
				}
				c.Hello = h
			}
			if m, ok := l73FirstHandshake(down, 2); ok {
				s, err := l73ParseServerHello(m)
				if err != nil {
					return nil, fmt.Errorf("%s: %w", c.Server, err)
				}
				c.ServerHello = s
			}
		case len(up) >= 12 && up[0]>>4 == 0xc:
			c.Plain = l73PlainHeaders(up)
		}
		out = append(out, *c)
	}
	return out, nil
}

// l73Stream is one direction's bytes in sequence order, overlaps dropped;
// it stops at the first gap, since what follows one was not captured.
func l73Stream(segs []l73Seg) []byte {
	if len(segs) == 0 {
		return nil
	}
	base := segs[0].seq
	for _, s := range segs {
		if int32(s.seq-base) < 0 {
			base = s.seq
		}
	}
	sort.SliceStable(segs, func(i, j int) bool { return segs[i].seq-base < segs[j].seq-base })
	var out []byte
	for _, s := range segs {
		off := int(s.seq - base)
		if off+len(s.data) <= len(out) {
			continue
		}
		if off > len(out) {
			break
		}
		out = append(out, s.data[len(out)-off:]...)
	}
	return out
}

type l73Rec struct {
	typ  uint8
	body []byte
}

func l73IsTLS(b []byte) bool { return len(b) >= 3 && b[0] == 22 && b[1] == 3 }

func l73Records(b []byte) []l73Rec {
	var out []l73Rec
	for len(b) >= 5 {
		n := int(binary.BigEndian.Uint16(b[3:5]))
		if len(b) < 5+n {
			break
		}
		out = append(out, l73Rec{b[0], b[5 : 5+n]})
		b = b[5+n:]
	}
	return out
}

// l73FirstHandshake is the body of the first handshake message in the
// stream's leading handshake records, if it is of type want.
func l73FirstHandshake(stream []byte, want uint8) ([]byte, bool) {
	var hs []byte
	for _, r := range l73Records(stream) {
		if r.typ != 22 {
			break
		}
		hs = append(hs, r.body...)
		if len(hs) >= 4 {
			n := int(hs[1])<<16 | int(hs[2])<<8 | int(hs[3])
			if len(hs) >= 4+n {
				if hs[0] != want {
					return nil, false
				}
				return hs[4 : 4+n], true
			}
		}
	}
	return nil, false
}

type l73Reader struct {
	b   []byte
	bad bool
}

func (r *l73Reader) bytes(n int) []byte {
	if n < 0 || len(r.b) < n {
		r.bad, r.b = true, nil
		return nil
	}
	v := r.b[:n]
	r.b = r.b[n:]
	return v
}

func (r *l73Reader) u8() uint8 {
	b := r.bytes(1)
	if b == nil {
		return 0
	}
	return b[0]
}

func (r *l73Reader) u16() uint16 {
	b := r.bytes(2)
	if b == nil {
		return 0
	}
	return binary.BigEndian.Uint16(b)
}

func (r *l73Reader) vec8() []byte  { return r.bytes(int(r.u8())) }
func (r *l73Reader) vec16() []byte { return r.bytes(int(r.u16())) }

func l73U16s(b []byte) []uint16 {
	var out []uint16
	for len(b) >= 2 {
		out = append(out, binary.BigEndian.Uint16(b))
		b = b[2:]
	}
	return out
}

func l73ParseClientHello(b []byte) (*l73Hello, error) {
	r := &l73Reader{b: b}
	h := &l73Hello{LegacyVersion: r.u16()}
	r.bytes(32) // random: read past, never kept
	r.vec8()    // legacy_session_id: read past, never kept
	h.Suites = l73U16s(r.vec16())
	r.vec8() // compression methods
	exts := &l73Reader{b: r.vec16()}
	if r.bad {
		return nil, errors.New("a ClientHello shorter than its fields")
	}
	for len(exts.b) > 0 && !exts.bad {
		typ := exts.u16()
		data := &l73Reader{b: exts.vec16()}
		h.Extensions = append(h.Extensions, typ)
		switch typ {
		case 0: // server_name
			list := &l73Reader{b: data.vec16()}
			for len(list.b) > 0 && !list.bad {
				nt, name := list.u8(), list.vec16()
				if nt == 0 {
					h.HasSNI, h.SNI = true, string(name)
				}
			}
		case 10:
			h.Groups = l73U16s(data.vec16())
		case 13:
			h.SigAlgs = l73U16s(data.vec16())
		case 16:
			list := &l73Reader{b: data.vec16()}
			for len(list.b) > 0 && !list.bad {
				h.ALPN = append(h.ALPN, string(list.vec8()))
			}
		case 41: // pre_shared_key: counted, never copied
			ids := &l73Reader{b: data.vec16()}
			for len(ids.b) > 0 && !ids.bad {
				ids.vec16()
				ids.bytes(4)
				h.PSKIdentities++
			}
		case 42:
			h.EarlyData = true
		case 43:
			h.Versions = l73U16s(data.vec8())
		case 45:
			for _, m := range data.vec8() {
				h.PSKModes = append(h.PSKModes, int(m))
			}
		}
	}
	if exts.bad {
		return nil, errors.New("a ClientHello extension shorter than its length")
	}
	return h, nil
}

// l73HRR is RFC 8446 §4.1.3's HelloRetryRequest random.
var l73HRR = []byte{0xCF, 0x21, 0xAD, 0x74, 0xE5, 0x9A, 0x61, 0x11, 0xBE, 0x1D, 0x8C, 0x02, 0x1E, 0x65, 0xB8, 0x91,
	0xC2, 0xA2, 0x11, 0x16, 0x7A, 0xBB, 0x8C, 0x5E, 0x07, 0x9E, 0x09, 0xE2, 0xC8, 0xA8, 0x33, 0x9C}

func l73ParseServerHello(b []byte) (*l73ServerHello, error) {
	r := &l73Reader{b: b}
	s := &l73ServerHello{Version: r.u16()}
	s.HelloRetry = bytes.Equal(r.bytes(32), l73HRR)
	r.vec8()
	s.Suite = r.u16()
	r.u8()
	if r.bad {
		return nil, errors.New("a ServerHello shorter than its fields")
	}
	if len(r.b) == 0 {
		return s, nil
	}
	exts := &l73Reader{b: r.vec16()}
	for len(exts.b) > 0 && !exts.bad {
		typ := exts.u16()
		data := &l73Reader{b: exts.vec16()}
		switch typ {
		case 43:
			s.Version = data.u16()
		case 41:
			s.PSKSelected = true
		}
	}
	if exts.bad || r.bad {
		return nil, errors.New("a ServerHello extension shorter than its length")
	}
	return s, nil
}

func l73PlainHeaders(b []byte) []l73Plain {
	var out []l73Plain
	for len(b) >= 12 {
		n := binary.BigEndian.Uint32(b[8:12])
		out = append(out, l73Plain{Type: b[1], SeqNo: b[2], Flags: b[3], Length: n})
		if uint64(len(b)) < 12+uint64(n) {
			break
		}
		b = b[12+n:]
	}
	return out
}

var (
	l73LongHex = regexp.MustCompile(`[0-9A-Fa-f]{32,}`)
	l73LongB64 = regexp.MustCompile(`[A-Za-z0-9+/_=-]{40,}`)
)

// l73SummaryLeaks names what a summary holds that it must not: a long hex
// or base64 run, which is the shape a ticket, an identity, a random or a key
// takes once printed. A run is INVALID if it names anything.
func l73SummaryLeaks(s string) []string {
	var hits []string
	if l73LongHex.MatchString(s) {
		hits = append(hits, "a long hex run")
	}
	if l73LongB64.MatchString(s) {
		hits = append(hits, "a long base64 run")
	}
	return hits
}

func l73Host(server string) string {
	h, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(server)
	if err != nil {
		return server
	}
	return h
}

func l73PortOf(server string) uint16 {
	_, p, err := net.SplitHostPort(server)
	if err != nil {
		return 0
	}
	n, _ := strconv.Atoi(p)
	return uint16(n)
}

// l73FirstPort is the port of the device's first connection to host; false
// when it opened none.
func l73FirstPort(conns []l73Conn, host string) (uint16, bool) {
	for _, c := range conns {
		if l73Host(c.Server) == host {
			return l73PortOf(c.Server), true
		}
	}
	return 0, false
}

// l73Window is the connections to host whose SYN falls in [from, to].
func l73Window(conns []l73Conn, host string, from, to time.Time) []l73Conn {
	var out []l73Conn
	for _, c := range conns {
		if l73Host(c.Server) == host && !c.SYNAt.Before(from) && !c.SYNAt.After(to) {
			out = append(out, c)
		}
	}
	return out
}

// l73Describe is conns as one line, times relative to from.
func l73Describe(conns []l73Conn, from time.Time) string {
	if len(conns) == 0 {
		return "the device opened no connection"
	}
	var parts []string
	for _, c := range conns {
		s := fmt.Sprintf("+%.2fs to %s: %s", c.SYNAt.Sub(from).Seconds(), c.Server, c.Answer)
		if c.ServerHello != nil {
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", %s %s", tls.VersionName(c.ServerHello.Version), tls.CipherSuiteName(c.ServerHello.Suite))
			if c.ServerHello.PSKSelected {
				s += " resumed"
			}
		}
		if c.DevicePayloadBytes > 0 {
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", device sent %d bytes", c.DevicePayloadBytes)
		}
		if c.ServerPayloadBytes > 0 {
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", server sent %d bytes", c.ServerPayloadBytes)
		}
		if !c.DeviceFINAt.IsZero() {
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", device fin at +%.2fs", c.DeviceFINAt.Sub(from).Seconds())
		}
		if !c.DeviceRSTAt.IsZero() {
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", device rst at +%.2fs", c.DeviceRSTAt.Sub(from).Seconds())
		}
		if c.DeviceRecords > 0 || c.ServerRecords > 0 {
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", %d device TLS records, %d server TLS records", c.DeviceRecords, c.ServerRecords)
		}
		if h := c.Hello; h != nil {
			s += ", client_hello (" + l73DescribeHello(h) + ")"
		}
		for _, a := range c.Alerts {
			who := "server"
			if a.FromDevice {
				who = "device"
			}
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", alert from %s level %d description %d (%s)", who, a.Level, a.Description, l73AlertName(a.Description))
		}
		if len(c.Plain) > 0 {
			var hdrs []string
			for _, h := range c.Plain {
				hdrs = append(hdrs, fmt.Sprintf("type %d seq_no %d flags 0x%02x", h.Type, h.SeqNo, h.Flags))
			}
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", %d plain TACACS+ header(s) (%s)", len(c.Plain), strings.Join(hdrs, "; "))
		}
		s += ", ended " + c.End
		if !c.EndAt.IsZero() {
			s += fmt.Sprintf(" at +%.2fs", c.EndAt.Sub(from).Seconds())
		}
		parts = append(parts, s)
	}
	return strings.Join(parts, "; ")
}

// l73DescribeHello names what the device offered: versions, suites, groups
// and signature algorithms (hex where Go has no name), whether it sent an SNI
// (never the name), the ALPN count, PSK identities and early data. It prints
// no key share, random or session ID.
func l73DescribeHello(h *l73Hello) string {
	name := func(v uint16, goName string, prefix string) string {
		if goName == "" || strings.HasPrefix(goName, prefix) {
			return fmt.Sprintf("0x%04x", v)
		}
		return goName
	}
	list := func(n int, f func(i int) string) string {
		var out []string
		for i := 0; i < n; i++ {
			out = append(out, f(i))
		}
		return "[" + strings.Join(out, " ") + "]"
	}
	versions := list(len(h.Versions), func(i int) string {
		v := h.Versions[i]
		if n := tls.VersionName(v); !strings.HasPrefix(n, "0x") {
			return n
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("0x%04x", v)
	})
	suites := list(len(h.Suites), func(i int) string {
		v := h.Suites[i]
		if n := tls.CipherSuiteName(v); !strings.HasPrefix(n, "0x") {
			return n
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("0x%04x", v)
	})
	groups := list(len(h.Groups), func(i int) string {
		v := h.Groups[i]
		return name(v, tls.CurveID(v).String(), "CurveID(")
	})
	sigs := list(len(h.SigAlgs), func(i int) string {
		v := h.SigAlgs[i]
		return name(v, tls.SignatureScheme(v).String(), "SignatureScheme(")
	})
	return fmt.Sprintf("versions %s; suites %s; groups %s; sig_algs %s; has_sni %v; alpn %d; psk_identities %d; early_data %v",
		versions, suites, groups, sigs, h.HasSNI, len(h.ALPN), h.PSKIdentities, h.EarlyData)
}

var l73AlertNames = map[uint8]string{
	0: "close_notify", 10: "unexpected_message", 20: "bad_record_mac", 22: "record_overflow", 40: "handshake_failure",
	42: "bad_certificate", 43: "unsupported_certificate", 44: "certificate_revoked", 45: "certificate_expired",
	46: "certificate_unknown", 47: "illegal_parameter", 48: "unknown_ca", 49: "access_denied", 50: "decode_error",
	51: "decrypt_error", 70: "protocol_version", 71: "insufficient_security", 80: "internal_error",
	86: "inappropriate_fallback", 90: "user_canceled", 109: "missing_extension", 110: "unsupported_extension",
	112: "unrecognized_name", 115: "unknown_psk_identity", 116: "certificate_required", 120: "no_application_protocol",
}

func l73AlertName(d uint8) string {
	if n, ok := l73AlertNames[d]; ok {
		return n
	}
	return "unnamed"
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run them and see them pass.** Run: `go test ./tests/lab/ -run '^TestL73' -count=1 -v`. Expected: every `TestL73…` passes. `TestL73ConnsReassemblesOutOfOrderDuplicates` should not skip on Go 1.26; if it does, say so in the report.

- [ ] **Step 5: Mutation check.** Run three mutations, one at a time, each against `go test ./tests/lab/ -run '^TestL73' -count=1`:
  1. In `l73Stream`, replace `out = append(out, s.data[len(out)-off:]...)` with `out = append(out, s.data...)`. `TestL73StreamDropsOverlapsAndStopsAtAGap` must fail (the overlap's bytes repeat). The `st.seen` dedupe in `l73Conns` is a second layer over the same duplicates, so deleting it alone stays green; that is expected and not a mutation to run.
  2. In `l73ParseClientHello`, replace the bare `r.vec8()` commented `legacy_session_id: read past, never kept` with `h.ALPN = append(h.ALPN, fmt.Sprintf("%x", r.vec8()))`. `TestL73ResumptionSummaryHoldsNoSessionBytes` must fail (the session id's hex is in the summary). Writing it into `h.SNI` would not do: the `server_name` extension parsed later overwrites `h.SNI`.

package lab

import (

func l73TestCSR(t *testing.T, key any, tmpl *x509.CertificateRequest) []byte {
	t.Helper()
	der, err := x509.CreateCertificateRequest(rand.Reader, tmpl, key)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return pem.EncodeToMemory(&pem.Block{Type: "CERTIFICATE REQUEST", Bytes: der})
}

func TestL73SignCSRCopiesTheRequestAndChainsToTheRoot(t *testing.T) {
	now := time.Now()
	for _, alg := range []string{"ecdsa", "rsa"} {
		pki, err := l73NewPKI(alg, now)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		key, _ := ecdsa.GenerateKey(elliptic.P256(), rand.Reader)
		csr := l73TestCSR(t, key, &x509.CertificateRequest{Subject: pkix.Name{CommonName: "srx1"},
			DNSNames: []string{"srx1"}, IPAddresses: []net.IP{net.ParseIP("172.100.100.21")}})
		cert, added, err := l73SignCSR(csr, pki.Inter, now.Add(-time.Minute), now.Add(time.Hour), "srx1", net.ParseIP("172.100.100.21"))
		if err != nil || added {
			t.Fatalf("%s: %v added=%v", alg, err, added)
		}
		roots, inters := x509.NewCertPool(), x509.NewCertPool()
		roots.AddCert(pki.Root.Cert)
		inters.AddCert(pki.Inter.Cert)
		if _, err := cert.Verify(x509.VerifyOptions{Roots: roots, Intermediates: inters, CurrentTime: now,
			KeyUsages: []x509.ExtKeyUsage{x509.ExtKeyUsageClientAuth}}); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("%s: %v", alg, err)
		}
		if _, err := cert.Verify(x509.VerifyOptions{Roots: roots, CurrentTime: now,
			KeyUsages: []x509.ExtKeyUsage{x509.ExtKeyUsageClientAuth}}); err == nil {
			t.Fatalf("%s: the leaf verified without its intermediate; cell (e)'s chain test would prove nothing", alg)
		}
		if !slices.Equal(cert.DNSNames, []string{"srx1"}) || len(cert.IPAddresses) != 1 {
			t.Fatalf("%s: %v %v", alg, cert.DNSNames, cert.IPAddresses)
		}
	}
}

func TestL73SignCSRAddsNamesOnlyWhenTheRequestHasNone(t *testing.T) {
	now := time.Now()
	pki, _ := l73NewPKI("ecdsa", now)
	key, _ := ecdsa.GenerateKey(elliptic.P256(), rand.Reader)
	csr := l73TestCSR(t, key, &x509.CertificateRequest{Subject: pkix.Name{CommonName: "srx1"}})

func TestL73CSRFactsReadWhatTheDeviceAsked(t *testing.T) {
	key, _ := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
	eku, _ := asn1.Marshal([]asn1.ObjectIdentifier{{1, 3, 6, 1, 5, 5, 7, 3, 1}})
	csr := l73TestCSR(t, key, &x509.CertificateRequest{Subject: pkix.Name{CommonName: "srx1"}, DNSNames: []string{"srx1"},
		ExtraExtensions: []pkix.Extension{{Id: asn1.ObjectIdentifier{2, 5, 29, 37}, Value: eku}}})
	f, err := l73CSRFacts(csr)
	if err != nil {

	if f.KeyAlg != "RSA-2048" || !slices.Equal(f.EKU, []string{"serverAuth"}) || !slices.Equal(f.DNSNames, []string{"srx1"}) ||

func TestL73CSRWithABadSignatureIsRefused(t *testing.T) {
	key, _ := ecdsa.GenerateKey(elliptic.P256(), rand.Reader)
	csr := l73TestCSR(t, key, &x509.CertificateRequest{Subject: pkix.Name{CommonName: "srx1"}})
	b, _ := pem.Decode(csr)
	b.Bytes[len(b.Bytes)-3] ^= 0xff
	if _, err := l73CSRFacts(pem.EncodeToMemory(b)); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("a tampered request read as good")
	}
}

func TestL73ServerCertificateNamesWhatItIsGiven(t *testing.T) {
	now := time.Now()
	pki, _ := l73NewPKI("ecdsa", now)
	c, err := pki.Server(pki.Root, l73ServerLeaf{IPs: []net.IP{net.ParseIP("172.100.100.1")},

	}
	if facts := l73CertFacts(c.Cert); !strings.Contains(facts, "iPAddress [172.100.100.1]") || !strings.Contains(facts, "EKU [serverAuth]") {
		t.Fatal(facts)
	}
	if k, err := c.KeyPEM(); err != nil || !strings.Contains(string(k), "PRIVATE KEY") {
		t.Fatalf("%v", err)
	}
}

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `tests/lab/l73_pki.go`:
```go
package lab

import (
	"crypto"
	"crypto/ecdsa"
	"crypto/elliptic"
	"crypto/rand"
	"crypto/rsa"
	"crypto/x509"
	"crypto/x509/pkix"
	"encoding/asn1"
	"encoding/pem"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"math/big"
	"net"
	"strings"
	"time"
)

// L73's PKI. The device and port49d both anchor at Root. The device's own
// certificate comes from Inter, so cell (e) can see whether the device
// sends its chain. Other is a CA neither side trusts. Every key is made
// here, held in memory, written only 0600 for port49d, and registered for
// the leak check; the device's key is made on the device and never leaves
// it.

type l73Cert struct {
	Cert *x509.Certificate
	Key  crypto.Signer
}

func (c l73Cert) PEM() []byte {
	return pem.EncodeToMemory(&pem.Block{Type: "CERTIFICATE", Bytes: c.Cert.Raw})
}

func (c l73Cert) KeyPEM() ([]byte, error) {
	der, err := x509.MarshalPKCS8PrivateKey(c.Key)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return pem.EncodeToMemory(&pem.Block{Type: "PRIVATE KEY", Bytes: der}), nil
}

func l73Key(alg string) (crypto.Signer, error) {
	switch alg {
	case "ecdsa":
		return ecdsa.GenerateKey(elliptic.P256(), rand.Reader)
	case "rsa":
		return rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
	}
	return nil, fmt.Errorf("key algorithm %q: want ecdsa or rsa", alg)
}

// l73Issue signs tmpl for pub with issuer; with issuer.Cert nil, tmpl is
// self-signed by issuer.Key.
func l73Issue(tmpl *x509.Certificate, pub crypto.PublicKey, issuer l73Cert) (*x509.Certificate, error) {
	sn, err := rand.Int(rand.Reader, new(big.Int).Lsh(big.NewInt(1), 126))
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	tmpl.SerialNumber = sn
	parent := issuer.Cert
	if parent == nil {
		parent = tmpl
	}
	der, err := x509.CreateCertificate(rand.Reader, tmpl, parent, pub, issuer.Key)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return x509.ParseCertificate(der)
}

func l73CA(alg, cn string, now time.Time, parent *l73Cert) (l73Cert, error) {
	key, err := l73Key(alg)
	if err != nil {
		return l73Cert{}, err
	}
	tmpl := &x509.Certificate{Subject: pkix.Name{CommonName: cn}, NotBefore: now.Add(-time.Hour),
		NotAfter: now.Add(48 * time.Hour), IsCA: true, BasicConstraintsValid: true,
		KeyUsage: x509.KeyUsageCertSign | x509.KeyUsageCRLSign | x509.KeyUsageDigitalSignature}
	signer := l73Cert{Key: key}
	if parent != nil {
		signer = *parent
	}
	cert, err := l73Issue(tmpl, key.Public(), signer)
	if err != nil {
		return l73Cert{}, err
	}
	return l73Cert{Cert: cert, Key: key}, nil
}

type l73PKI struct {
	Alg                string
	Root, Inter, Other l73Cert
}

func l73NewPKI(alg string, now time.Time) (*l73PKI, error) {
	root, err := l73CA(alg, "L73 Lab Root", now, nil)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	inter, err := l73CA(alg, "L73 Lab Device Intermediate", now, &root)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	other, err := l73CA(alg, "L73 Untrusted CA", now, nil)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return &l73PKI{Alg: alg, Root: root, Inter: inter, Other: other}, nil
}

// l73ServerLeaf is one server certificate port49d presents in a cell.
type l73ServerLeaf struct {
	DNSNames            []string
	IPs                 []net.IP
	NotBefore, NotAfter time.Time
}

func (p *l73PKI) Server(from l73Cert, l l73ServerLeaf) (l73Cert, error) {
	key, err := l73Key(p.Alg)
	if err != nil {
		return l73Cert{}, err
	}
	tmpl := &x509.Certificate{Subject: pkix.Name{CommonName: "port49 L73"}, DNSNames: l.DNSNames, IPAddresses: l.IPs,
		NotBefore: l.NotBefore, NotAfter: l.NotAfter, KeyUsage: x509.KeyUsageDigitalSignature,
		ExtKeyUsage: []x509.ExtKeyUsage{x509.ExtKeyUsageServerAuth}}
	if p.Alg == "rsa" {
		tmpl.KeyUsage |= x509.KeyUsageKeyEncipherment
	}
	cert, err := l73Issue(tmpl, key.Public(), from)
	if err != nil {
		return l73Cert{}, err
	}
	return l73Cert{Cert: cert, Key: key}, nil
}

// l73CSR is what a device's certificate request asks for.
type l73CSR struct {
	Subject   string
	DNSNames  []string
	IPs       []string
	Emails    []string
	KeyAlg    string
	Requested []string // extension OIDs the request carries, dotted
	EKU       []string // extended key usages it asks for; none when empty
}

var l73OIDEKU = asn1.ObjectIdentifier{2, 5, 29, 37}

func l73ParseCSR(csrPEM []byte) (*x509.CertificateRequest, error) {
	b, _ := pem.Decode(csrPEM)
	if b == nil || !strings.Contains(b.Type, "CERTIFICATE REQUEST") {
		return nil, errors.New("no PEM certificate request")
	}
	csr, err := x509.ParseCertificateRequest(b.Bytes)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if err := csr.CheckSignature(); err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("the request's signature does not verify: %w", err)
	}
	return csr, nil
}

func l73CSRFacts(csrPEM []byte) (l73CSR, error) {
	csr, err := l73ParseCSR(csrPEM)
	if err != nil {
		return l73CSR{}, err
	}
	f := l73CSR{Subject: csr.Subject.String(), DNSNames: csr.DNSNames, Emails: csr.EmailAddresses, KeyAlg: l73KeyName(csr.PublicKey)}
	for _, ip := range csr.IPAddresses {
		f.IPs = append(f.IPs, ip.String())
	}
	for _, e := range csr.Extensions {
		f.Requested = append(f.Requested, e.Id.String())
		if e.Id.Equal(l73OIDEKU) {
			var oids []asn1.ObjectIdentifier
			if _, err := asn1.Unmarshal(e.Value, &oids); err != nil {
				return f, fmt.Errorf("the requested extended key usage: %w", err)
			}
			for _, o := range oids {
				f.EKU = append(f.EKU, l73EKUName(o.String()))
			}
		}
	}
	return f, nil
}

func l73EKUName(oid string) string {
	switch oid {
	case "1.3.6.1.5.5.7.3.1":
		return "serverAuth"
	case "1.3.6.1.5.5.7.3.2":
		return "clientAuth"
	}
	return oid
}

func l73KeyName(pub any) string {
	switch k := pub.(type) {
	case *rsa.PublicKey:
		return fmt.Sprintf("RSA-%d", k.N.BitLen())
	case *ecdsa.PublicKey:
		return "ECDSA-" + k.Curve.Params().Name
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("%T", pub)
}

// l73SignCSR is the lab CA's answer to the device's request: its subject and
// names as asked, clientAuth (what port49 requires, decision 50 ruling 7).
// A request that names neither a dNSName nor an iPAddress cannot name the
// device to port49, so name and addr are added and added says so.
func l73SignCSR(csrPEM []byte, issuer l73Cert, notBefore, notAfter time.Time, name string, addr net.IP) (*x509.Certificate, bool, error) {
	csr, err := l73ParseCSR(csrPEM)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, false, err
	}
	tmpl := &x509.Certificate{Subject: csr.Subject, DNSNames: csr.DNSNames, IPAddresses: csr.IPAddresses,
		EmailAddresses: csr.EmailAddresses, NotBefore: notBefore, NotAfter: notAfter,
		KeyUsage: x509.KeyUsageDigitalSignature, ExtKeyUsage: []x509.ExtKeyUsage{x509.ExtKeyUsageClientAuth}}
	if _, ok := csr.PublicKey.(*rsa.PublicKey); ok {
		tmpl.KeyUsage |= x509.KeyUsageKeyEncipherment
	}
	added := len(csr.DNSNames) == 0 && len(csr.IPAddresses) == 0
	if added {
		tmpl.DNSNames, tmpl.IPAddresses = []string{name}, []net.IP{addr}
	}
	cert, err := l73Issue(tmpl, csr.PublicKey, issuer)
	return cert, added, err
}

// l73CertFacts is a certificate as one note line.
func l73CertFacts(c *x509.Certificate) string {
	var ips, ekus []string
	for _, ip := range c.IPAddresses {
		ips = append(ips, ip.String())
	}
	for _, u := range c.ExtKeyUsage {
		switch u {
		case x509.ExtKeyUsageClientAuth:
			ekus = append(ekus, "clientAuth")
		case x509.ExtKeyUsageServerAuth:
			ekus = append(ekus, "serverAuth")
		default:
			ekus = append(ekus, fmt.Sprint(int(u)))
		}
	}
	if len(ekus) == 0 {
		ekus = []string{"none"}
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("subject %q, issuer %q, dNSName %v, iPAddress %v, EKU %v, key %s, valid %s to %s",
		c.Subject.String(), c.Issuer.String(), c.DNSNames, ips, ekus, l73KeyName(c.PublicKey),
		c.NotBefore.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339), c.NotAfter.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339))
}
```
