### Task 10: `TestL73`, part 3: cells (i), (j), (b) and the note

**Files:**
- Modify: `tests/lab/l73_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `bridgeDevice()` (harness), `freePort(t)` (`l72_test.go`), Task 8's env.
- Produces: `cellI`, `cellJ`, `cellB`, `note`, `addSecondAddress`.

- [ ] **Step 1: The second address.**
```go
// addSecondAddress puts 172.100.100.2 on the clab bridge beside .1, as
// prepareSplit does, and takes it off when the test ends.
func (e *l73Env) addSecondAddress() {
	if e.bridge {
		return
	}
	dev, err := bridgeDevice()
	if err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("INVALID: %v", err)
	}
	del := func() { _ = exec.Command("sudo", "-n", "ip", "addr", "del", l73SecondServer+"/24", "dev", dev).Run() }
	del()
	// Registered before the add: every exit path, a Fatal, an INVALID or the
	// STOP guard included, takes the address off again.
	e.t.Cleanup(del)
	if out, err := exec.Command("sudo", "-n", "ip", "addr", "add", l73SecondServer+"/24", "dev", dev).CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("INVALID: adding %s to %s: %v\n%s", l73SecondServer, dev, err, out)
	}
	e.bridge = true
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Cell (i).** The device lists a second TLS server, 172.100.100.2:300, where nothing listens (an RST). Each refusal is met with a break-glass login: port49 holds no token for `breakglass`, so the login lands only if the device reaches its local method. The timeline comes from the capture. A row's verdict needs a port49 record of its own reason (`tls_outcome`, `diagnosis` or top-level `reason`); without one the row is unproven and contradicts nothing. `i-error` is an observation only: spec §5.7 `store_unavailable` records from L4 that an authentication ERROR is final on Junos with `tacplus` alone, so a failed local login there contradicts no ruling. `i-window`'s second login must land too.
```go
// cellI: the device lists a second TLS server, 172.100.100.2:300, where
// nothing listens (an RST). Each refusal is met with a break-glass login:
// port49 holds no token for breakglass, so the login lands only if the device
// reaches its local method. The timeline comes from the capture. A row's
// verdict needs a port49 record of the row's own reason; without one the row
// says the cause is unproven and contradicts nothing.
func (e *l73Env) cellI() {
	e.guard()
	e.addSecondAddress()
	d := e.base()
	d.Second, d.SecondTLS, d.SecondPort = l73SecondServer, true, e.port
	e.configure(d)
	fault := e.write("fault", []byte("store_unavailable\n"))
	unreachable := fmt.Sprintf("{crls: [{name: l73, url: \"http://127.0.0.1:%d/l73.crl\", issuer_file: %s}]}", freePort(e.t), e.files["inter.pem"])
	load, _ := os.ReadFile("/proc/loadavg")
	refusals := []struct {
		id, what, outcome string
		o                 l73Daemon
		again             bool
	}{
		{"i-alert", "an alert at the handshake (client_ca_file a CA that did not issue the device's chain: unknown_ca)", "unknown_ca",
			l73Daemon{ClientCA: e.files["other.pem"]}, false},
		{"i-accept", "a reset at Accept (the device's record says transport tcp: transport_mismatch)", "transport_mismatch",
			l73Daemon{Inventory: e.invTCP, Intermediates: e.files["inter.pem"]}, false},
		{"i-window", "a decline window (certificate status unavailable, an unreachable CRL: status_unavailable), then a second login at once, inside the window",
			"status_unavailable", l73Daemon{Intermediates: e.files["inter.pem"], Status: unreachable}, true},
		{"i-error", "an ERROR reply (the lab fault switch, store_unavailable: authentication answers ERROR)", "store_unavailable",
			l73Daemon{Intermediates: e.files["inter.pem"], FaultFile: fault}, false},
	}
	var parts, unproven []string
	docs := "yes: decision 50 rulings 5, 7 and 8 for the rows whose break-glass login landed (i-alert, i-accept, i-window); " +
		"i-error is an observation only: spec §5.7 store_unavailable records from L4 that an authentication ERROR is final on Junos with tacplus alone"
	contradicted := false
	for _, r := range refusals {
		e.guard()
		e.settle()
		e.revive(r.id)
		e.daemon(r.id, r.o)
		stop := e.capture(r.id)
		off, start := e.l.mark(), time.Now()
		ok, took, err := e.breakglassLogin()
		s := fmt.Sprintf("%s: break-glass login ok %v in %s (%s)", r.what, ok, took.Round(time.Millisecond), l73Err(err))
		ok2 := true
		if r.again {
			var took2 time.Duration
			var err2 error
			ok2, took2, err2 = e.breakglassLogin()
			s += fmt.Sprintf(", the second ok %v in %s (%s)", ok2, took2.Round(time.Millisecond), l73Err(err2))
		}
		conns := stop()
		e.reached(r.id, conns)
		recs := e.l.recordsSince(off)
		n := len(l73Where(recs, "tls_outcome", r.outcome)) + len(l73Where(recs, "diagnosis", r.outcome))
		for _, rec := range recs {
			if str(rec, "reason") == r.outcome {
				n++
			}
		}
		proof := fmt.Sprintf("%d record(s) of %s", n, r.outcome)
		switch contra, proven := l73RefusalVerdict(r.id, n, ok, ok2, r.again); {
		case !proven:
			proof = "no record of " + r.outcome + ", so the refusal itself is unproven"
			unproven = append(unproven, r.id)
		case contra != "":
			contradicted = true
			docs = contra
		}
		parts = append(parts, fmt.Sprintf("%s; %s; %s: %s", s, l73Describe(conns, start), proof, e.quote(recs)))
	}
	e.configure(e.base())
	if len(unproven) > 0 && !contradicted {
		docs = fmt.Sprintf("n/a: no port49 record proves the refusal on %v, so those rows say nothing about ruling 8", unproven)
	}
	e.add("i", fmt.Sprintf("servers 172.100.100.1:%d (TLS, port49d refusing as each row says) then 172.100.100.2:%d (TLS, "+
		"nothing listening: an RST); login method list %s; load average at the start %s", l73TLSPort, l73TLSPort,
		e.d.Fallback, strings.TrimSpace(string(load))),
		strings.Join(parts, "; ")+". An ERROR for an obfuscated packet (decision 50 ruling 13) cannot be provoked from port49's "+
			"side: only a device that clears the flag earns it, and cell b counts whether this one ever did.", docs)
}

// l73RefusalVerdict grades one cell (i) row. proven needs a port49 record of
// the row's own reason (n of them). contra is the docs text of a proven row
// whose device did not reach its local method, "" otherwise. i-error is an
// observation only: spec §5.7 store_unavailable records from L4 that an
// authentication ERROR is final on Junos with tacplus alone, so a failed
// local login there is the expected behaviour and contradicts no ruling.
// i-window's second login, inside the window, must land too (ruling 8).
func l73RefusalVerdict(id string, n int, ok, ok2, again bool) (contra string, proven bool) {
	if n == 0 {
		return "", false
	}
	if id == "i-error" {
		return "", true
	}
	ruling := map[string]string{
		"i-alert":  "7 (a chain with no path to the anchors is refused)",
		"i-accept": "5 (what needs no TLS byte is decided at Accept)",
		"i-window": "8 (an unavailable status declines the device)",
	}[id]
	switch {
	case !ok:
		return "**no**: decision 50 ruling " + ruling + " leaves the device to its local method; on " + id + " the local login did not land", true
	case again && !ok2:
		return "**no**: decision 50 ruling " + ruling + " sends the next login, inside the window, to the local method; on " + id + " the login inside the window did not land", true
	}
	return "", true
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Cell (j).**
```go
func (e *l73Env) cellJ() {
	e.guard()
	e.addSecondAddress()
	// j1: TLS only, port49 refusing, its RFC 8907 listener on .1:49 open.
	e.settle()
	e.revive("j1")
	e.daemon("j1", l73Daemon{ClientCA: e.files["other.pem"]})
	stop := e.capture("j1")
	start := time.Now()
	ok1, took1, err1 := e.breakglassLogin()
	c1 := stop()
	e.reached("j1", c1)
	plain1 := 0
	for _, c := range c1 {
		if l73PortOf(c.Server) == 49 {
			plain1++
		}
	}
	// j2: a mixed list, TLS .1 then plain .2, a second server address (j's
	// same-host question is j1's; Juniper documents a mixed list, and on IOS
	// XE and NX-OS it is the same group with a plain server after the TLS
	// one), port49's RFC 8907 listener moved to .2:49.
	// A device that refuses the list is an observation, not an INVALID run:
	// NX-OS's `tacacs-server secure tls` may apply to every host.
	e.settle()
	d := e.base()
	d.Second, d.SecondTLS = l73SecondServer, false
	e.touched = true
	if out, err := e.apply(d); err != nil {
		if _, rerr := e.restore(); rerr != nil && e.d.Family == "junos" {
			e.t.Fatalf("INVALID: restoring after the refused mixed list: %s", oneLine(hideKeys(rerr.Error())))
		}
		e.configure(e.base())
		refusal := l73Refusal(out)
		if refusal == "" {
			refusal = oneLine(hideKeys(err.Error()))
		}
		e.add("j", "j1: the TLS server alone, port49d refusing its handshake, port49d's RFC 8907 listener open on 172.100.100.1:49; "+
			"j2: TLS 172.100.100.1 then plain 172.100.100.2",
			fmt.Sprintf("j1: break-glass ok %v in %s (%s), connections to port 49: %d; %s. j2: the device refused the mixed "+
				"list: %s", ok1, took1.Round(time.Millisecond), l73Err(err1), plain1, l73Describe(c1, start), refusal),
			l73JDocs(plain1))
		return
	}
	e.revive("j2")
	e.daemon("j2", l73Daemon{ClientCA: e.files["other.pem"], Listen: net.JoinHostPort(l73SecondServer, "49")})
	stop = e.capture("j2")
	off, start2 := e.l.mark(), time.Now()
	ok2, took2, err2 := e.breakglassLogin()
	c2 := stop()
	e.reached("j2", c2)
	recs := e.l.recordsSince(off)
	var headers int
	for _, c := range c2 {
		if l73Host(c.Server) == l73SecondServer && l73PortOf(c.Server) == 49 {
			headers += len(c.Plain)
		}
	}
	mismatch := 0
	for _, r := range l73Where(recs, "diagnosis", "transport_mismatch") {
		if str(sub(r, "unvalidated"), "transport") == "" { // the RFC 8907 listener's record carries no transport
			mismatch++
		}
	}
	e.configure(e.base())
	docs := l73JDocs(plain1)
	e.add("j", "j1: the TLS server alone, port49d refusing its handshake, port49d's RFC 8907 listener open on 172.100.100.1:49; "+
		"j2: a mixed list, TLS 172.100.100.1 then plain 172.100.100.2 (a second server address; port49d's RFC 8907 listener moved there), the TLS handshake refused",
		fmt.Sprintf("j1: break-glass ok %v in %s (%s), connections to port 49: %d; %s. j2: break-glass ok %v in %s (%s); %s; plain "+
			"TACACS+ headers the device sent to 172.100.100.2:49: %d; port49 declined it transport_mismatch %d time(s) (decision 50 "+
			"ruling 4); records: %s",
			ok1, took1.Round(time.Millisecond), l73Err(err1), plain1, l73Describe(c1, start), ok2, took2.Round(time.Millisecond),
			l73Err(err2), l73Describe(c2, start2), headers, mismatch, e.quote(recs)), docs)
}

// l73JDocs reads j1: a TLS-only list must never reach port 49.
func l73JDocs(plain1 int) string {
	if plain1 > 0 {
		return "**no**: RFC 9887 §5.1.1 forbids falling back to non-TLS for the same server; with a TLS-only list the device opened port 49"
	}
	return "yes: RFC 9887 §5.1.1"
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Cell (b).** This cell reads every record the run made.
```go
// cellB: port49d answers any packet whose unencrypted flag is clear with an
// ERROR and records obfuscated_over_tls (decision 50 ruling 13), so no such
// record across every login of the run means every packet had the flag
// set. The capture cannot show it: the header is inside TLS. With no TLS
// record at all the run proves nothing either way.
func (e *l73Env) cellB() {
	e.guard()
	recs := e.l.recordsSince(e.start)
	obf := len(l73Where(recs, "diagnosis", "obfuscated_over_tls"))
	byEvent := map[string]int{}
	total := 0
	for _, r := range recs {
		if str(sub(r, "device"), "transport") == "tls" && str(r, "event") != "unvalidated" {
			byEvent[str(r, "event")]++
			total++
		}
	}
	docs := "yes: RFC 9887 §4, decision 50 ruling 13"
	switch {
	case obf > 0:
		docs = "**no**: RFC 9887 §4 requires the flag on every packet over TLS"
	case total == 0:
		docs = "n/a: port49 recorded no validated TLS packet, so the flag is unproven"
	}
	e.add("b", "every TLS login of this run (cells a, c, d, e, g, h); port49d checks each packet's flag",
		fmt.Sprintf("obfuscated_over_tls records: %d; records over TLS by event: %v", obf, byEvent), docs)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The note.**
```go
// note reads the device's configuration, gates every evidence file, writes
// the evidence, the L71 addendum and the L73 note. Nothing reaches
// results/ or evidence/ before this, so an INVALID run writes nothing.
func (e *l73Env) note() {
	e.guard()
	var conf string
	e.cli(func(c *cliConn) error {
		for _, cmd := range e.d.ShowCmds {
			o, err := c.sendExpect(cmd, 60*time.Second, e.opMark(c))
			if err != nil {
				return err
			}
			conf += o + "\n"
		}
		return nil
	})
	e.evidence["device-config.txt"] = []byte(l71MaskExchange(l73ScrubLog(hideKeys(conf))))
	all := e.l.recordsSince(e.start)
	var lines []string
	for _, r := range all {
		b, _ := json.Marshal(r)
		lines = append(lines, string(l72RedactCredentialHash(b)))
	}
	e.evidence["decisions.jsonl"] = []byte(strings.Join(lines, "\n") + "\n")
	secrets := e.allSecrets()
	for name, body := range e.evidence {
		if len(labcreds.Contains(body, secrets)) > 0 {
			e.t.Fatalf("INVALID: evidence %s holds a secret; nothing written", name)
		}
		if strings.HasSuffix(name, ".txt") && len(l72TranscriptLeaks(string(body))) > 0 {
			e.t.Fatalf("INVALID: evidence %s carries TLS session material; nothing written", name)
		}
		if strings.HasSuffix(name, ".tls.json") && len(l73SummaryLeaks(string(body))) > 0 {
			e.t.Fatalf("INVALID: evidence %s holds a long hex or base64 run; nothing written", name)
		}
	}
	table, err := l73RenderTable(e.obs)
	if err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("INVALID: %v", err)
	}
	var b strings.Builder
	b.WriteString("### Run\n\n")
	for _, f := range e.facts {
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "- %s\n", f)
	}
	attrs := e.d.Attrs
	if attrs == "" {
		attrs = "the lab file's"
	}
	fmt.Fprintf(&b, "- port49d: `certificate_status: {disabled: true}` in every cell but (i)'s decline window (the status path is "+
		"L72's), `malformed_backoff_base: 2s`, `session_attributes: %s`; the device: %s\n", attrs, e.d.Revocation)
	fmt.Fprintf(&b, "- not measured here: SCEP, CMPv2, EST and ACME enrolment; whether %s checks port49's revocation status\n\n", e.d.Vendor)
	b.WriteString("### Cells\n\n" + table)
	note := string(l72RedactCredentialHash([]byte(b.String())))
	if len(labcreds.Contains([]byte(note), secrets)) > 0 {
		e.t.Fatal("INVALID: the note holds a secret; nothing written")
	}
	if err := os.RemoveAll(e.evDir); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if err := os.MkdirAll(e.evDir, 0o755); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	for name, body := range e.evidence {
		if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(e.evDir, name), body, 0o644); err != nil {
			e.t.Fatal(err)
		}
	}
	e.writeL71()
	ev := "tests/lab/evidence/L73-" + e.l.p.name + "/"
	e.l.captureNote = fmt.Sprintf("`%s` — per-cell TLS summaries (`<cell>.tls.json`: each connection's ClientHello and "+
		"ServerHello fields, plaintext alerts, record counts and timings, with no session byte by construction; the raw "+
		"captures stay git-ignored in `tests/lab/captures/L73-%s/`), the decision log (`decisions.jsonl`, credential hashes "+
		"redacted), the device's TACACS+/TLS log lines and configuration (masked), and the CLI help exchange (`l71-probe.txt`).",
		ev, e.l.p.name)
	e.l.result(e.t, "L73", l73Claim(e.d.Vendor), note)
	e.l.captureNote = ""
	var contra []string
	for _, o := range e.obs {
		if strings.HasPrefix(o.Docs, "**no**") {
			contra = append(contra, o.ID)
		}
	}
	if len(contra) > 0 {
		e.t.Logf("cells %v contradict a document: CLAUDE.md rule 4, the doc is wrong (Task 17 fixes it)", contra)
	}
}
```
`writeL71` writes its probe file into `e.evDir`, which `note` has just written in full, so the order above (evidence, then `writeL71`) keeps both.

### Task 11: The run procedure, and the vJunos-router run

**Amendments from the vJunos-router run (attempts 3 to 14).** The harness code in the blocks above was brought in line with what the run needed. In short:

- **Refusals are read, not assumed.** `junosLineRefusal` (cli_test.go) decides, per typed line, whether the device refused it: an `error:`, `syntax error` or `unknown command` anywhere in its answer (checked before `[edit]` is allowed, so `[edit]` cannot hide an error), and for a `set` line any output beyond the echo, a blank line, `[edit…` and the prompt. `junosVerifyApplied` then reads the committed configuration back (`show configuration <hierarchy> | display set`, the first two words of each `set` line) and names every typed line missing from it; the secret line is compared by its words before `secret` and printed masked.
- **The scrub shows one thing it used to mask.** `l73ScrubLog` still drops any line naming a password, token, secret or JWT first. A line that is only a printable path (`l73BarePath`, as `file list` heads a listing), and the path inside Junos's `File path '<p>' doesn't exist` answer (`l73FilePathAnswer`), is kept unmasked when `l73PrintablePath` allows it: under `/var`, `/config`, `/etc`, `/tmp` or `/opt`, characters `A-Za-z0-9_./-` only, at most 200 long, no segment over 64 and no `.` or `..` segment. The text around the path is still masked (`l73MaskLine`). `l73RefusedDir` names the directory of such a path under `/var` or `/config`; `refusalDiag` lists it with `file list`.
- **A refused configure keeps evidence.** `configure` writes the screen, then `refusalDiag` reads the dialect's `RefusalCmds` (and the refused directory) into `PORT49_L73_INVALID_DIR`, all through `invalidScreen`. `InvalidCmds` do the same for a failed control login. `aaaListing` records `/var/db/aaa_certs/` before and after each trust step and does nothing unless that directory variable is set.
- **A help diagnostic exists and is off by default.** With `PORT49_L73_DIAG=help`, `diagHelp` (first in cell (a)) types `?` at each of the dialect's `DiagOpAsks` (operational mode) and `DiagAsks` (configuration mode, at a value position), clears the line with Ctrl-U, keeps the masked help, and ends INVALID before any TLS line is typed. It never presses Enter.
- **The trust group is committed after the CA certificates load** (`l73TrustGroupLines`, with a leading `delete`). Attempt 12 showed the order is not what the image objected to; it is kept because it is harmless and pinned by a test.
- **The trust folder is PROVISIONAL.** The image looks for a TACACS+ `tls trusted-ca-group` at `/var/db/aaa_certs/tacplus_tls/<group>`. Juniper's TACACS+ over TLS pages are silent on what that is. The RADSEC page ("Configure RADIUS over TLS (RADSEC) for System Authentication", https://www.juniper.net/documentation/us/en/software/junos/user-access/topics/topic-map/user-access-radius-authentication.html) describes a trusted-ca-group as a folder of CA certificates each named `<openssl subject hash>.0`, and the TACACS+ overview (tacacs-over-tls-overview.html) is the page that says nothing, so `loadJunos` builds that folder by analogy: `file make-directory`, then `file copy` of the root and intermediate public certificates it uploaded (`l73TrustFolderCmds`, an allowlist of those two paths). Attempt 14 measured the first command refused for the break-glass user (`Permission denied`: the directory needs root or `start shell`). The step fails closed, so every Junos enrol is INVALID until Ryan rules on the vJunos root shell (progress.md, "Task 11 attempt 14", HOLD). Do not re-run it blind, do not move a private key off the device to get past it, and do not edit the step to hide the refusal.

Tasks 11–16 each run **procedure P** below for one opt-in row. One selector per run.

| Task | Selector | Platform name | Container | Build (Task 1) | Deadline |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | `vjunos26` | `juniper_vjunos-router` | `clab-port49-vjunos26-srx1` | done in Task 1 | 150 min |

**Procedure P** (`SEL`, `NAME`, `CONT` and `MIN` from the table):

- [ ] **P1: Preconditions.** Unprivileged checks only. Run each, and on any surprise stop and report; never stop, signal or kill a process this run did not start:
```sh
test ! -e /tmp/port49-STOP-LAB && echo no-sentinel
df -BM / | tail -1                                                # Avail >= 67584M
flock -n /tmp/port49-lab.lock true && echo lock-free || echo "lab occupied: $(cat /tmp/port49-lab.lock)"
docker ps --filter label=containerlab --format '{{.Names}}'       # expect nothing
ss -ltn '( sport = :49 or sport = :300 )'                         # expect no listener on 172.100.100.1, 0.0.0.0 or *
```
A Junos run also needs `openssl` on the host (`command -v openssl`): the trust folder's file names are `openssl x509 -noout -subject_hash`, and without it the run is INVALID at enrol.
If the lock is held, wait for its holder (plan 11a's lab tasks take the same lock) and re-run P1; never break it. If something listens on :49 or :300 on the bridge address, report it with `pgrep -a -x port49d` (a listing, nothing more). The harness's own "already listening" error suggests `sudo ss` and `pkill -f`; do neither.

- [ ] **P2: A pinned run worktree** at the branch head, under `.worktrees/` (not `/tmp`):
```sh
SHA=$(git -C /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/l73-junos rev-parse HEAD)
git -C /home/rwendt/git/port49 worktree add --detach /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73 "$SHA"
cd /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73 && make build
```

- [ ] **P3: Build the test binary and start the run in the background** (Bash `run_in_background: true`). The run is a binary this procedure starts, so its PID is recorded at launch, and nothing later finds it by pattern:
```sh
R=/home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73
cd "$R" && go test -c -tags lab -o "$R/lab.test" ./tests/lab/ && mkdir -p /tmp/claude-l73-invalid && cd "$R/tests/lab" && {
  PORT49_L73_INVALID_DIR=/tmp/claude-l73-invalid PORT49_LAB_PLATFORMS=$SEL \
    "$R/lab.test" -test.run '^TestL73$' -test.count=1 -test.v -test.timeout 3h \
    > /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73-$SEL.log 2>&1 &
  echo $! > "$R/lab.pid"; wait $!; }
```
The binary runs from `tests/lab`, as `go test` would run it. The run's log can echo device output, so read it only through the lab rule's filter:
`grep -v -i 'password\|token\|secret\|eyJ' <log> | sed -E 's/[A-Za-z0-9_+\/=.-]{24,}/<LONG>/g' | tail -40`.

- [ ] **P4: Arm both watchdogs at once** (Bash `run_in_background: true`). Write the script to the scratchpad once (Task 11), then reuse it:
```sh
cat > "$SCRATCH/l73-watch.sh" <<'EOF'
#!/bin/sh
# l73-watch.sh <run-worktree> <platform-name> <container> <deadline-minutes>
# Exits 0 when the run's lab.test exits or the sentinel appears, 1 on a
# 10-minute stall or past the deadline. It watches one process, the PID P3
# recorded, and only while that PID is still the run's binary. It watches
# mtimes and byte counts only; it prints nothing a capture or log holds.
W=$1; P=$2; C=$3; END=$(( $(date +%s) + $4 * 60 )); last=$(date +%s)
PID=$(cat "$W/lab.pid")
ours() { [ "$(readlink /proc/"$PID"/exe 2>/dev/null)" = "$W/lab.test" ]; }
sig() {
  m=$(stat -c %Y "$W"/tests/lab/captures/L73-"$P"/*.pcap "$W"/tests/lab/logs/decisions-"$P".jsonl \
        "$W"/tests/lab/logs/port49d-"$P".log 2>/dev/null | sort -n | tail -1)
  n=$(docker logs --since 2m "$C" 2>&1 | wc -c)
  echo "$m:$n"
}
prev=$(sig)
while :; do
  sleep 120
  ours || { echo "lab.test ($PID) exited"; exit 0; }
  [ -e /tmp/port49-STOP-LAB ] && { echo "STOP sentinel present"; exit 0; }
  now=$(date +%s); cur=$(sig)
  case "$cur" in *:0) ;; *) [ "$cur" != "$prev" ] && last=$now ;; esac
  prev=$cur
  [ $((now - last)) -gt 600 ] && { echo "STALL: no progress for $((now - last))s (lab.test $PID)"; exit 1; }
  [ "$now" -gt "$END" ] && { echo "DEADLINE passed (lab.test $PID)"; exit 1; }
done
EOF
chmod +x "$SCRATCH/l73-watch.sh"
"$SCRATCH/l73-watch.sh" /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73 $NAME $CONT $MIN
```
`$SCRATCH` is the session's scratchpad directory.
- Re-arm the watchdog after every wake.
- On a STALL, take a goroutine dump of that one process and nothing else: `PID=$(cat $R/lab.pid); [ "$(readlink /proc/$PID/exe)" = "$R/lab.test" ] && kill -QUIT "$PID"`. Then diagnose; do not wait out the timeout. Never `pgrep -f` or `pkill`: plan 11a's `make test` runs a `lab.test` of its own.
- When lab.test exits, arm a second watcher (20 min) for this task's commit.

- [ ] **P5: Read the outcome.**
  - **PASS:** `results/L73-$NAME.md` and `evidence/L73-$NAME/` exist in the run worktree, and the L71 note has its addendum (or, on a platform never probed before, `results/L71-$NAME.md` exists). On a help-only row, PASS is the L71 note alone.
  - **"L73 not measured … no RFC 9887 client":** only the L71 addendum is written. That is a valid negative. Go to P7a–P7d with it alone, and in Task 17 record the L71 observation only.
  - **INVALID, a stall or the deadline:** nothing is written. Run P7a (captures out) and P7d (remove the run worktree) **before anything else**, so the next `worktree add` finds no worktree and loses no capture. Then read `/tmp/claude-l73-invalid/` (scrubbed screens) and the filtered log, fix the harness on `test/l73-junos` (a commit), and re-pin from P2. Typical fixes:
    - an enrolment prompt the dialogue does not know: a new `l73Step` in Task 6a's table;
    - a keyword the image spells differently: the dialect's lines and asks;
    - "evidence `…-device-log.txt` carries TLS session material": an IOS XE or NX-OS log line names a PSK or a session id, and `l72TranscriptLeaks` refuses the bare words. Narrow the dialect's `LogCmd` so those lines are not read. Never weaken the gate.

    A control failure under ECDSA: re-run with `PORT49_L73_ALG=rsa`, and keep the ECDSA run's INVALID message for the commit body.
  - Record the measured boot time: the log's `lab: <name>: ready after …` line.

- [ ] **P6: Replace the boot placeholder.** On `test/l73-junos`, set this row's boot budget in `harness_test.go` to about 3× the measured ready time. Rewrite its comment to state the measurement and the date, the way the older rows do.

- [ ] **P7a: Captures out (every exit path).** PASS, negative, INVALID, stall or deadline, this runs before the run worktree is removed. Every attempt is archived under its own stamp; only a PASS replaces the main checkout's copy, which the labpcap audit reads:
```sh
R=/home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73; A=/mnt/images/port49-l73-2026-10-17
STAMP=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ); mkdir -p "$A"
[ -d "$R"/tests/lab/captures/L73-$NAME ] && cp -r "$R"/tests/lab/captures/L73-$NAME "$A"/L73-$NAME-$STAMP
# PASS or the negative only:
# rm -rf /home/rwendt/git/port49/tests/lab/captures/L73-$NAME && mkdir -p /home/rwendt/git/port49/tests/lab/captures \
#   && cp -r "$R"/tests/lab/captures/L73-$NAME /home/rwendt/git/port49/tests/lab/captures/
```

- [ ] **P7b: The secret gate** (PASS or the negative only). It reads only the files this run wrote under `results/` and `evidence/` (for a note the run appended to, only the appended lines), and compares them with secret **values**: the run's own private keys line by line, the lab's published key and break-glass password, and the shapes of a crypt hash or Junos `$9$` string (`$1$`, `$5$`, `$6$`, `$9$`, any salt length), a JWT and a PEM private key. It prints file names, never a matched line. A hit on help text quoting `$9$-encrypted` errs safe: report it, never weaken the pattern. Write it once (Task 11):
```sh
cat > "$SCRATCH/l73-gate.sh" <<'EOF'
#!/bin/sh
# l73-gate.sh <run-worktree> <platform-name> [help]: names every file this run
# wrote under results/ or evidence/ that holds a secret value. Prints no
# content. "help": a help-only row, which makes no keys.
R=$1; N=$2; umask 077; T=$(mktemp -d); trap 'rm -rf "$T"' EXIT
cat "$R"/tests/lab/logs/l73-"$N"/*.key 2>/dev/null | grep -v -e '-----' -e '^$' > "$T/values"
[ -s "$T/values" ] || [ "$3" = help ] || { echo "no key files in logs/l73-$N: the gate has nothing of the run's to compare"; exit 1; }
printf '%s\n' labsecret breakglass-only >> "$T/values"
git -C "$R" status --porcelain --untracked-files=all -- tests/lab/results tests/lab/evidence > "$T/files"
[ -s "$T/files" ] || { echo "the run wrote nothing under results/ or evidence/"; exit 1; }
hit=0
while read -r st f; do
  case "$st" in
    '??'|A) [ -r "$R/$f" ] || { echo "UNREADABLE $f" >&2; hit=1; continue; }; cat "$R/$f" ;;
    *) git -C "$R" diff --no-color -U0 -- "$f" | grep '^+' | grep -v '^+++' ;;
  esac > "$T/text"
  grep -q -F -f "$T/values" "$T/text"; r1=$?
  grep -q -E -e '-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE' -e '\$[0-9][a-z]?\$[^[:space:]]{8,}' -e 'eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}' "$T/text"; r2=$?
  grep -q -P '(?<!cert)_sha256"?\s*[:=]\s*\[?"?[0-9a-f]{64}' "$T/text"; r3=$?
  if [ $r1 -ne 1 ] || [ $r2 -ne 1 ] || [ $r3 -ne 1 ]; then
    echo "SECRET VALUE (or a grep error) in $f"; hit=1
  fi
done < "$T/files"
exit $hit
EOF
chmod +x "$SCRATCH/l73-gate.sh"
"$SCRATCH/l73-gate.sh" /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73 $NAME && echo gate-clean
```
On a help-only row (Task 15) pass a third argument, `help`. It must print `gate-clean` and nothing else. A hit makes the run INVALID: copy nothing (P7c), remove the worktree (P7d), and report the file name. The seed tokens are not on disk after the run; `note()`'s in-process gate (`allSecrets`) compares every one of them before anything is written.

- [ ] **P7c: Copy the results out** (PASS or the negative, after `gate-clean`):
```sh
R=/home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73; B=/home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/l73-junos
[ -d "$R"/tests/lab/evidence/L73-$NAME ] && cp -r "$R"/tests/lab/evidence/L73-$NAME "$B"/tests/lab/evidence/
cp "$R"/tests/lab/results/L71-$NAME.md "$B"/tests/lab/results/
[ -f "$R"/tests/lab/results/L73-$NAME.md ] && cp "$R"/tests/lab/results/L73-$NAME.md "$B"/tests/lab/results/
```
Then run P7a's commented PASS lines. If the L71 note now holds two TestL73 addenda for the same image (a re-run after an earlier committed one), delete the older block: the new run's addendum stands, and the commit says so.

- [ ] **P7d: Remove the run worktree.**
```sh
git -C /home/rwendt/git/port49 worktree remove --force /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/run-l73
```

- [ ] **P8: Commit.**
```bash
cd /home/rwendt/git/port49/.worktrees/l73-junos
git add tests/lab/results/L71-$NAME.md tests/lab/evidence/L73-$NAME tests/lab/harness_test.go tests/lab/README.md
[ -f tests/lab/results/L73-$NAME.md ] && git add tests/lab/results/L73-$NAME.md
git commit -m "docs: record L73 on <version> (row stays open)

Measured with TestL73 at <SHA>, PKI <alg>. <one line per cell that reads
**no** against a document, or: no cell contradicts a document; or, for a
help cell: the L71 reading.> The <selector> boot budget is now the
measured <ready time> x3. README: the image's build line.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

### Task 14: The cEOS-lab 4.36.3M run

**Amendments from the cEOS run (attempts 1 to 3, help diagnostics).** The image's help showed no `ssl-profile` on the host line (`key`, `port`, `single-connection`, `timeout`, `tls`, `vrf`); after `tls` only `port`, `single-connection`, `timeout`, `vrf` are offered, so a TLS host carries no key. The profile is global: `tacacs-server tls ssl-profile` ("Configure global SSL profile to use"). Inside `ssl profile` the image lists `certificate`, `chain`, `peer`, `trust`, `tls` and more; `certificate` offers `WORD`, `chain certificate` and `trust certificate` offer `WORD`. `PORT49_L73_DIAG=help` gained `DiagDeep`, `DiagFilter`/`DiagFilterAsks` and `DiagSub*` to read these. The undo adds `no tacacs-server tls ssl-profile`. Attempts 4 and 5: with no port configured the TLS host defaulted to port 300 (`show tacacs`: `172.100.100.1/300 TLS`, cell a's answer) while the AAA group member `server 172.100.100.1` is port 49, so the device never connected. The group members are now typed after the hosts, as `server <addr> tls port <P>` (`tls` only on a TLS host, as the member's help lists it; attempt 6 showed a plain member under a TLS host opens no connection; P: the configured port, else the port `show tacacs` lists for that host, never assumed; `l73EOSMemberPorts`), the help ask at the member needs `port`, the undo names the port (`no server <addr> port <P>`), and the read-back also requires `show tacacs` to list each host and a server-group member at `<addr>/<P>` carrying the host's `TLS` tag (`l73EOSGroupMissing`). Every help exchange is also kept in the INVALID dir (`probe-N`).

**Review fixes after the cEOS PASS (3a19c3da was dropped from the branch before any push).** The note and `decisions.jsonl` had committed 1,240 and 16,864 lab seed `mint` records whose `token_sha256` is an array (`["<hex>"]`), which the string-only redaction missed. Now: (a) the evidence and the `e.quote` records leave out the lab seed mints and say how many (`l73DropSeedMints`); (b) `l72RedactCredentialHash` masks `token_sha256` and `credential_sha256` as a string or as an array; (c) `l72HashValueLeaks` fails the note and every evidence file in-process on any 64-hex value under a `*_sha256` key (not `cert_sha256`, a public fingerprint), and the P7b gate script (`l73-gate.sh`) does the same with `grep -P`, proven on the array, string, `cert_sha256` and redacted shapes. Also: offsets print `%+.2fs` and a connection opened before the window is tagged as the readiness probe; the EOS apply and undo remove both server addresses as `tls` and plain hosts (cell i's TLS second host had survived into j2), and the read-back refuses a host the cell did not type; j2 says the configuration did not read back, not that the device refused, unless a refusal screen exists; cell d grades a refusal from the fatal alert the device sent in the clear (capture `plaintext_alerts`), names it in the row, and reads yes against RFC 9887 section 3.4 when all four rows were refused.

**Task 15 IOL-XE help asks (changed after the first probe).** The 17.18.02 IOL image has no `tacacs server <name>` object, so its help is asked in the legacy form and nothing is entered but `configure terminal`: (1) `tacacs-server host 192.0.2.71 ?` (L71's verdict; needs `tls`), (2) `tacacs-server ?` (its Want words are a control: whatever it lists is recorded), (3) `tacacs-server host 192.0.2.71 tls ?` only if (1) listed `tls`. The 2026-10-18 run found (1) prints `<cr>` only, which is a valid negative (`l73CROnly`); the dialect now lists four asks in this order: the host ask, `tacacs-server ?` and config-mode `?` (kept to the lines beginning `aaa` or `tacacs`) as evidence-only asks that run even when (1) finds no client, then the gated `tls` level. The finding states only what was seen. The XRd retry copies `docker logs` into the INVALID directory through the same scrub as the help screens before the container is destroyed.

- [ ] **Step 1: The image.** Run `docker image inspect ceos:4.36.3M --format '{{.Id}} {{.Size}}'`. If the image is missing, do not fail. Write "cEOS skipped: `ceos:4.36.3M` not present on <date>" in the report and the README's L73 image list, commit that line (`docs: L73 cEOS image not present; skipped`), and go to Task 15. L73 then stays open (Task 17). Otherwise record the ID and the size in the README's image list.
- [ ] **Step 2: Run procedure P for `ceos436`.** Its L71 note is new: `results/L71-arista_ceos.md`. The vEOS 4.31.2F note stays as it is. Its L73 note is `results/L73-arista_ceos.md`.
- [ ] **Step 3: If bring-up fails** `waitDeviceReady` or `waitTACACSReady`, read `show interfaces Management0` and `show running-config section tacacs` over `docker exec clab-port49-ceos436-leaf1 Cli -p 15 -c '<cmd>'`. That container is the lab's own. Pass the output through the log filter. If Management0 has no address, `configs/eos-startup.cfg` was not merged with a management interface as Task 2 assumed. Report that before changing anything: a `configs/ceos-startup.cfg` is a harness change, with its own commit.
- [ ] **Step 4: If the SSL profile is refused** (INVALID at `loadEOS`, with the screen in `/tmp/claude-l73-invalid/`), correct `l73EOSEnrol`'s profile lines from the screen and the image's own help. Enter `management security`, then `ssl profile x`, then type `?`, and leave with `abort` if EOS offers it, otherwise with `no ssl profile x` and `end`. Commit, then re-run.

- [ ] **Step 6: The lab README.** After the L72 section, add:
```markdown
## L73: device RFC 9887 clients against port49

`PORT49_LAB_PLATFORMS=<selector> go test -tags lab ./tests/lab/ -run '^TestL73$' -count=1 -v -timeout 3h`,
one selector per run (`vjunos26`, `iosxe1718`, `nxos106`, `ceos436`; help cells `iolxe1718`, `xrd261`;
`vjunosswitch26`), from a pinned worktree under `.worktrees/`, after `make build`.

- **Opt-in selectors.** A run that names no platform never deploys them.
- **Occupancy.** The test takes the lab's single-occupant lock (`/tmp/port49-lab.lock`,
  `labOccupyForRun`), holds it until `TestMain` has torn the topology down, and refuses to start
  beside another containerlab topology.
- **Order of a run.** It first asks the image's help about every statement it types (an addendum
  to L71). It then enrols the device: the key pair stays on the device, a Junos request is read
  from a file, and an IOS XE, NX-OS or EOS request is read off the terminal. Last, it runs cells
  (a)–(j) against port49d, restarted per cell with a `tls_listener:`.
- **Dialects.** Per-platform lines, asks and enrolment prompts live in `l73_dialect.go`
  (`l73Dialects`). An unknown prompt is INVALID, with the scrubbed screen in
  `PORT49_L73_INVALID_DIR`.
- **Evidence.** Raw captures stay git-ignored in `captures/L73-<platform>/`, outside
  `make evidence`'s glob: TLS is not `labpcap`'s. The committed evidence is
  `evidence/L73-<platform>/<cell>.tls.json`, a per-connection summary whose types hold no session
  bytes. Beside it are the redacted decision log, the device's masked configuration and TLS log
  lines, and the help exchange.
- **PKI.** `PORT49_L73_ALG=rsa` switches the lab PKI from ECDSA P-256 to RSA 2048.
```
